3.5.
Coisson algebras

Definition 3.30
.

A
coisson
algebra is a pair
(A,\{\cdot,\cdot\})
, where
A\in{\textup{Comm}}^{!}({D}(X))
and

\{\cdot,\cdot\}\in P_{2}^{*}(\{A,A\},A)\penalty\ ,

is a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket such that the adjoint action of
A
on
A
is an action of a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
-algebra on a
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra by derivations, as in Definition
3.23
. Said more prosaically,
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
must satisfy the Leibniz rule in each slot.

One should note the similarity to the definition of a Poisson algebra—hence the nomenclature.

Coisson algebras also admit a description as algebras internal to the so-called classical operad. Let
Q(I)
denote the set of equivalence relations on
I
and identify each element,
\sim
, with the corresponding pair
(\pi_{S},S)
for
\pi_{S}:I\rightarrow S=I/\sim
. We use
I_{s}
to denote the fibre,
\pi_{S}^{-1}(s)\subset I
over
s\in S
.

We recall the following the definitions from 1.4.27 of
[
BD04
]
:

Definition 3.31
.

The
classical
pseudo-tensor structure on
{D}(X)
, denoted as
{D}(X)^{c}
, is defined by

P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\bigoplus_{(\pi_{S},S)\in Q(I)}P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)_{S}\penalty\ ,

where for each
\pi_{S}:I\to S
we define

P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)_{S}=P_{S}^{*}\big(\{\otimes!_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}\}_{s\in S},M\big)\otimes\big(\otimes_{s\in S}{\textup{Lie}}(|I_{s}|)\big)\penalty\ ,

for any
L_{i},M\in{D}(X)
. The composition maps are defined in
1.4.27
of
[
BD04
]
.

There is a natural, auxiliary grading on the space of classical operations

P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{|I|-1}P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle p\rangle}\penalty\ ,

where

(3.3)

P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle p\rangle}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\pi_{S},S)\in Q(I)\\
|S|=p+1\end{subarray}}P_{S}^{*}(\{\otimes^{!}_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}\},M)\otimes(\otimes_{s\in S}{\textup{Lie}}(|I_{s}|))\ ,

with the direct sum running over quotients,
S
, of
I
whose cardinality is
p+1
.

In particular, we have

P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle 0\rangle}=P^{!}_{I}(\{L_{i}\},M)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}(|I|)\qquad\text{and}\qquad P_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle|I|-1\rangle}=P^{*}_{I}(\{L_{i}\},M)\ ,

and so one should, heuristically, think of the auxiliary grading as counting the number of
*
-operations appearing at a given arity. This gives rise to the following maps of coloured operads:

(3.4)

{D}(X)^{!}\otimes^{H}{\textup{Lie}}\xrightarrow{\alpha}{D}(X)^{c}\xrightarrow{\beta}{D}(X)^{*}

extending the identity functor on
{D}(X)
, where
\otimes^{H}
denotes the Hadamard tensor product of
{\mathbb{K}}
-linear operads. Similarly, the following are proved in
[
BD04
]
:

Lemma 3.32
.

For any coloured operad
\mathcal{P}
, there is a natural isomorphism

{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(\mathcal{P}\otimes^{H}{\textup{Lie}})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}({\textup{Lie}},\mathcal{P}\otimes^{H}{\textup{Lie}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}({\textup{Comm}},\mathcal{P})={\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Comm}}}(\mathcal{P})

Proof.

This is proved in section 1.1.10 in
[
BD04
]
.
∎

Proposition 3.33
.

The category
{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{c})
is equivalent to that of (non-unital) coisson algebras, such that the induced functors

{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{!}\otimes{\textup{Lie}})\to{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{c})\to{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{*})

correspond to the inclusion of
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebras as coisson algebras with trivial
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket, and the forgetful functor on coisson algebras remembering only the underlying
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket, respectively.

Proof.

This is precisely Lemma 1.4.28 of
[
BD04
]
, with some aspects of the proof explicitly stated.
∎

Concretely, at the level of binary operations, the preceding Proposition
3.33
follows from the fact that

(3.5)

P_{2}^{c}(\{L,L\},L)=\left(P_{2}^{!}(\{L,L\},L)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}(2)\right)\oplus P_{2}^{*}(\{L,L\},L)\penalty\ ,

where the two summands correspond to the two equivalence relations on the set
\{1,2\}
, which have
p=0
and
p=1
in the above grading, respectively, and thus the 2-ary Lie bracket structure map internal to
P_{2}^{c}(\{L,L\},L)
consists of precisely a commutative 2-ary operation internal to
{D}(X)^{!}
and an alternating 2-ary operation internal to
{D}(X)^{*}
.

From the definition, we have a forgetful functor

{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{c})\rightarrow{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{*})

from coisson algebras to
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras. If
L
is a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra, then the
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra
\textup{Sym}(L)
has a unique coisson bracket such that the inclusion
L\hookrightarrow\textup{Sym}(L)
is a morphism of
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra—this is the Kirillov–Kostant–Souriau bracket. Indeed,

\textup{Sym}:{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{*})\rightarrow{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}({D}(X)^{c})\penalty\ ,

is left adjoint to the forgetful functor.

The endomorphism operad of an object
M\in{D}(X)^{c}
can be described concretely by the following:

Proposition 3.34
.

The spaces of operations of
{\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(M)\in\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}
are given by

{\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(M)(n)=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n-1}\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}{\textup{Ind}}_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{1}}\times...\times{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{p+1}}}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\left(P_{p+1}^{*}(\{M^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{p+1},M)\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})\right)\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\ .

Proof.

Recall the auxiliary grading on
{D}(X)^{c}
,

{\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(M)(n)=P_{n}^{c}(\{M\}_{i=1}^{n},M)=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n-1}P^{c}_{n}(\{M\}_{i=1}^{n},M)^{\langle p\rangle}\ ,

where
P^{c}_{n}(\{M\}^{n}_{i=1},M)^{\langle p\rangle}
denotes the sum over quotients of cardinality
p+1
. Fix an ordering so that each quotient of cardinality
p+1
may be identified with
\{1,\dots,p+1\}
. Summing over all quotients of cardinality
p+1
is then almost the same as summing over all surjections
\{1,\dots,n\}{\ \twoheadrightarrow\ }\{1,\dots,p+1\}
, except we should take
{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}
-coinvariants to account for the choice of ordering.

The group
{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}
acts on the space of such surjections with equivalence classes labelled by the set of decompositions
n_{1}+\dots+n_{p}=n
. The stabilizer of the standard representative of such an equivalence class is
{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{1}}\times\dots\times{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{p+1}}
. Summing over all surjections is equivalent to summing over all decompositions but accounting for the size of the orbit and so we obtain

P^{c}_{n}(\{M\},M)^{\langle p\rangle}=\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}{\textup{Ind}}_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{1}}\times...\times{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{p+1}}}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\bigg(P_{p+1}^{*}(\{M^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{p+1},M)\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})\bigg)\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\ .

∎

4.
Algebras of the vertex kind

Fix
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
, as our algebraic curve. It is known, see Section
0.15
of
[
BD04
]
, that weakly translation invariant chiral, coisson or
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
recover the more traditional notions of vertex algebra, vertex Poisson algebra, and vertex Lie algebra, respectively. In this section, we review the definitions of the three aforementioned algebras and spell out this equivalence, following the exposition in Appendix A of
[
BDSHK19
]
.

The additive group
\mathbb{G}_{a}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
by translations and we have an equivalence of categories,

{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}\simeq{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}

where
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
is the ring of polynomials in the indeterminate
{{\partial}}
. The functor
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}\rightarrow D(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}
is given by

V\mapsto V\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\penalty\ ,

with D-module structure defined by letting the section
z\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
act by multiplication on
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
and letting
\partial_{z}\in\Theta_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
act as
\partial-\partial_{z}
. The inverse functor is given by taking the fibre at zero.

4.1.
Preliminaries and conventions

4.1.1.
Commutative algebras with derivation

The category
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod is symmetric monoidal with the tensor product
\otimes=\underset{{\mathbb{K}}}{\otimes}
and the action of
\partial
given by the coproduct
\partial\mapsto\partial\otimes 1+1\otimes\partial
. We write
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!}
for the pseudo-tensor structure induced by this tensor product.

Proposition 4.1
.

A commutative algebra internal to
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!}
is a commutative algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}
equipped with an action of
\partial
by derivations.

The forgetful functor
{\textup{Comm}}({\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!})\rightarrow{\textup{Comm}}(\Vect_{\mathbb{K}})
admits a left adjoint which sends a commutative
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra
A
to the jet algebra
{\mathcal{J}}A\in{\textup{Comm}}({\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!})
.
Indeed, the corresponding schemes
{\textup{Spec\ }}{\mathcal{J}}A
are an interesting class of weakly translation invariant
{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
-schemes.

4.1.2.
Vertex Lie algebras

We recall the definition of a vertex Lie algebra, also known as a Lie conformal algebra or (rarely) simply a conformal algebra.

Definition 4.2
.

A vertex Lie algebra is a pair
(L,[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot])
consisting of an
L\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod and a
\lambda
-bracket

[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]:L\otimes L\rightarrow L[\lambda]\penalty\ ,

satisfying

(i)

[{a}_{\lambda}b]=-[{a}_{-\lambda-{{\partial}}}b]

(skewsymmetry)

(ii)

[\partial{a}_{\lambda}b]=-\lambda[{a}_{\lambda}b]
and
[{a}_{\lambda}\partial b]=(\lambda+\partial)[{a}_{\lambda}b]

(sesquilinearity)

(iii)

[a_{\lambda}[b_{\mu}c]]-[c_{\mu}[a_{\lambda}b]]=[[a_{\lambda}b]_{\lambda+\mu}c]

(Jacobi identity)

for
a,b,c\in L
.

4.1.3.
Vertex algebras

We adopt the following, somewhat unconventional, definition of a vertex algebra, first appearing in the work of
[
DSK06
]
.

Definition 4.3
.

A (non-unital) vertex algebra is a pair
(V,I_{\lambda})
consisting of an
L\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod and a so-called integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}:V\otimes V\rightarrow V[\lambda]\penalty\ ,

satisfying

(i)

\partial I_{\lambda}(a,b)=I_{\lambda}(\partial a,b)+I_{\lambda}(a,\partial b)
and
\tfrac{d}{d\lambda}I_{\lambda}(\partial a,b)=\lambda\tfrac{d}{d\lambda}I_{\lambda}(a,b)

(sesquilinearity)

(ii)

I_{\lambda}(a,b)=I_{-\lambda-\partial}(b,a)

(skewsymmetry)

(iii)

I_{\lambda}(a,I_{\mu}(b,c))-I_{\mu}(b,I_{\lambda}(a,c))-I_{\lambda+\mu}(I_{\lambda}(a,b),c)=0

(Jacobi identity)

A
unital
vertex algebra is a vertex algebra
(V,I_{\lambda})
with a distinguished vector
\ket{0}\in\ker\partial\subset V
such that, for any
a\in V

I_{\lambda}(a,\ket{0})=a\penalty\ .

Remark 4.4
.

Often, one splits the integral
\lambda
-bracket into two operations

:\,\,:\,\,\,=I_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)|_{\lambda=0}\penalty\ ,\text{ and }[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]=\frac{d}{d\lambda}I_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)

where
[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]
is an honest
\lambda
-bracket on
V
, making it a vertex Lie algebra, and
:\,\,:
, the normally ordered product, is a noncommutative, nonassociative binary operation.

Remark 4.5
.

The usual definition of a vertex algebra structure on
L\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod consists of an integer family of products
\{_{(n)},n\in\mathbb{Z}\}

{}_{(n)}:L\otimes L\rightarrow L\penalty\ ,

satisfying some conditions. One can recover the products from the integral
\lambda
-bracket as

{a}_{(n)}b=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{n!}\tfrac{d^{n+1}}{d\lambda^{n+1}}I_{\lambda}(a,b)|_{\lambda=0}\penalty\ ,\text{ for }n\geq 0\\
\partial^{1+n}I_{\lambda}(a,b)|_{\lambda=0}\penalty\ ,\text{ for }n<0\end{cases}\penalty\ .

The usual conditions on the products are equivalent to the conditions
(i)
–
(iii)
for
I_{\lambda}
, see
[
DSK06
]
.

Remark 4.6
.

We say that a subspace
W\subset V

strongly generates

V
if any vector in
V
can be written as a linear combination of normally ordered monomials

:\partial^{i_{1}}w_{1}:\partial^{i_{2}}w_{2}:_{\dots\dots}:\partial^{i_{k-1}}w_{k-1}\partial{i_{k}}w_{k}:_{\dots\dots}:

for
w_{i}\in W
. In the literature, one usually says that a
set
strongly generates
V
if the
w_{i}
above can be chosen amongst this set—clearly any basis of
W
gives a strongly generating set of
V
.

4.1.4.
Vertex Poisson algebras

Definition 4.7
.

Let
V\in{\textup{Comm}}({\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!})
,
i.e.
V
is a commutative
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra with derivation
{{\partial}}
. A vertex Poisson algebra structure on
V
is a
\lambda
-bracket

[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]:V\otimes V\rightarrow V[\lambda]\penalty\ ,

such that

(i)

[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]
defines a vertex Lie algebra structure on the underlying
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-module of
V
,

(ii)

[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]
distributes over the commutative product on
V
via

•

[{a}_{\lambda}bc]=[{a}_{\lambda},b]c+[{a}_{\lambda}c]b

(right Leibniz rule)

•

[a{b}_{\lambda}c]=[{a}_{\lambda+\partial}c]_{\rightarrow}b+[{b}_{\lambda+\partial}c]_{\rightarrow}a

(left Leibniz rule)

where the arrow subscript means that
{{\partial}}
acts to the right of the bracket.

A rich source of vertex Poisson algebras comes from ordinary Poisson algebras and vertex Lie algebras as explained in the propositions below.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
Y
be a Poisson variety over
{\mathbb{K}}
, then
{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathcal{J}}Y]
has a unique vertex Poisson algebra structure such that

[a_{\lambda}b]=\{a,b\}\penalty\ ,

where
a,b\in{\mathbb{K}}[Y]\subset{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathcal{J}}Y]
and
\{\cdot,\cdot\}
is the Poisson bracket on
{\mathbb{K}}[Y]
.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
(L,[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot])
be a vertex Lie algebra then
\textup{Sym}(L)
has a unique vertex Poisson algebra structure given by the bracket
\{\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot\}
such that

\{a_{\lambda}b\}=[a_{\lambda}b]\penalty\ ,

for
a,b\in L=\textup{Sym}^{1}(L)\subset\textup{Sym}(L)
.

4.2.
Chiral algebras on the line and vertex algebras

Note that the equivalence
{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}\simeq{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}
lifts to an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories

{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{!}\simeq{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}^{!}

The following proposition follows immediately from unravelling the definitions.

Proposition 4.10
.

A weakly translation invariant
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, i.e., a commutative algebra internal to
{D}(\mathbb{A}^{1})^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w,!}
is equivalent to a commutative algebra in
{\mathbb{K}}
-Mod, equipped with a derivation.

For the rest of this subsection, we fix a finite set
I\in{\textup{fSet}}
and an
I
-family
L_{i}\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}
, for
i\in I
, with corresponding
I
-family of weakly translation invariant D-modules
\widetilde{L_{i}}\in{D}(X)^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}
. Fix also a
M\in{\mathbb{K}}[{{\partial}}]{\textup{-Mod}}
with corresponding D-module
\widetilde{M}\in{D}(X)^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}
.

Let
\Delta^{(I)}:\mathbb{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{I}
denote the closed embedding of the smallest diagonal and let
j^{(I)}:U^{(I)}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{I}
denote the open embedding of the complement of the union of all diagonals.

4.2.1.
The star pseudotensor structure

We start with defining the star pseudo-tensor structure. The global sections of
\Delta^{(I)}_{*}\widetilde{M}
can be computed in terms of
M
as

\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{I},\Delta^{(I)}_{*}\widetilde{M})\cong M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[z,\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{I})\textup{-Mod}\penalty\ ,

where
\partial_{z_{i}}
acts as
-\lambda_{i}
and
z_{i}
acts as
z-\partial_{\lambda_{i}}
. For
L_{i}\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod with associated D-modules
\widetilde{L}_{i}\in{D}(X)^{\mathbb{G}_{a},w}
, we have the isomorphisms

\displaystyle\Hom_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{I})}\big(\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{I},{{\boxtimes}}_{i\in I}\widetilde{L}_{i}),\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{I},\Delta_{*}\widetilde{M}))\big)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{I})}\big(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i}[z_{i}],\,M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[z,\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\big)\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial_{i}|i\in I]}\big(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i},M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[z,\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\big)\penalty\ .

where
\partial_{i}
acts on
L_{i}
and
\partial_{i}
acts as
\partial_{z_{i}}
on the codomain. An element
t\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
acts on the codomain by
z\mapsto z+t
. Thus, the translation-invariant morphisms are precisely those whose images land in the subspace
M\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\subset M\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\partial}}]}{\mathbb{K}}[z,\lambda_{i}|i\in I]
.

Definition 4.11
.

The star pseudo-tensor structure on
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod, denoted as
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}^{*}
, is defined by having multilinear operations

P^{*,\mathbb{G}_{a}}(\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},M)=\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial_{i}|i\in I]}(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i},M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{i}|i\in I])\penalty\ .

We forgo defining the composition of operations but note that in the case of a single colour the compositions are defined in
[
BDSHK19
]
.

Proposition 4.12

(
[
BD04
,
BDSHK19
]
)
.

A vertex Lie algebra is a Lie algebra internal to
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}^{*}
, or equivalently a weakly translation invariant
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Proof.

The subspace of binary operations on a single colour
L
is

P_{2}^{*}(\{L,L\},L)=\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}\big(L\otimes L,L[\lambda]\big)\penalty\ ,

where we identify
L\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}]
with
L[\lambda]
by
\lambda_{1}=\lambda
and
\lambda_{2}=-\partial-\lambda
. A Lie algebra structure on
L
is fixed by choosing some
b\in P_{2}^{*}(\{L,L\},L)
satisfying the Jacobi relation.

One recognises
P_{2}^{*}(\{L,L\},L)
as the space of
\lambda
-brackets satisfying conditions
(i)
and
(ii)
of Definition
4.2
,
i.e.
, skewsymmetry and sesquilinearity. Condition
(iii)
being equivalent to
b\in P_{2}^{*}(\{L,L\},L)
satisfying the Jacobi relation is the content of Proposition 5.1 of
[
BDSHK19
]
.
∎

4.2.2.
The chiral pseudotensor structure

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star}={\mathcal{O}}(U^{(I)})
be the co-ordinate ring of the complement of the union of all diagonals in
\mathbb{A}^{I}
, then
{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star}\in{D}(\mathbb{A}^{I})
.

Then,

\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{I},j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I),*}(\boxtimes_{i\in I}\widetilde{L}_{i}))\cong\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{I})\textup{-Mod}\penalty\ ,

with
z_{i}
acting on the right on
{\mathcal{O}}_{n}^{\star}
by multiplication and
\partial_{z_{i}}
acting as
\partial^{(i)}+\partial_{z_{i}}
, where
\partial^{(i)}
acts on
L_{i}
. Therefore, we have an isomorphism

\Hom_{D(\mathbb{A}^{I})}(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}(\boxtimes_{i\in I}L),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)\cong\Hom_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{n})}\big(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star},M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[z,\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\big)\penalty\ .

Proposition 4.13

(
[
BDSHK19
]
)
.

The subspace of translation invariant morphisms can be identified with

\Hom_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{I})^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}}\big(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star,\mathbb{G}_{a}},M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\big)\penalty\ ,

Proof.

This is Lemma A.1 of
[
BDSHK19
]
.
∎

Definition 4.14
.

The chiral pseudo-tensor structure on
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-Mod, denoted by
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}^{\rm ch}
, is defined by having space of multilinear operations

P^{ch}_{I}(\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},M)=\Hom_{{\mathcal{D}}(\mathbb{A}^{I})^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}}\big(\underset{i\in I}{\otimes}L_{i}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{I}^{\star,\mathbb{G}_{a}},M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{i}|i\in I]\big)\penalty\ .

Proposition 4.15

(
[
BD04
,
BDSHK19
]
)
.

A vertex algebra is a Lie algebra internal to the operad
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\textup{-Mod}^{\rm ch}
.

Proof.

The subspace of binary operations on a single colour
L\in{\mathbb{K}}[{{\partial}}]{\textup{-Mod}}
is

P^{{\rm ch}}_{2}(\{L,L\},L)=\Hom_{\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{A}^{2})^{\mathbb{G}_{a}}}(L\otimes L\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[(z_{1}-z_{2})^{\pm 1}],L[\lambda])\penalty\ ,

where we identify
\lambda_{1}=\lambda
and
\lambda_{2}=-\partial-\lambda
. Any such morphism is fully determined by the image of
L\otimes L\otimes(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}
. Let
\mu\in P_{2}^{{\rm ch}}(\{L,L\},L)
; for
l_{1},l_{2}\in L
, define a putative integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}(l_{1},l_{2})=\mu(l_{1},l_{2},(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1})\penalty\ .

That
I_{\lambda}
satisfies the axioms of an integral
\lambda
-bracket if and only if
\mu
satisfies the Jacobi relation is, essentially, the content of Theorem
6.12
of
[
BDSHK19
]
.
∎

4.2.3.
The classical pseudotensor structure

Recall that a vertex Poisson algebra is a vertex Lie algebra structure on a commutative algebra with derivation—satisfying a distributivity law. In light of the preceding discussion, we can recast this as a weakly translation invariant
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
structure on a weakly translation invariant
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Proposition 4.16
.

A vertex Poisson algebra is a weakly translation invariant coisson algebra on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

As before let
Q(I)
denote the set of equivalence relations on
I
and we enumerate its elements by
(\pi_{S},S)
, where
S=I/\sim
and
\pi_{S}:I\rightarrow S
is the projection.

Definition 4.17
.

The classical pseudotensor structure on
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}
, denoted as
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{c}
has spaces of multilinear operations

P^{c}_{I}(\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},M)=\bigoplus_{(\pi_{S},S)\in Q(I)}P^{c}_{I}(\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},M)_{S}

where

P^{c}_{I}(\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},M)=P_{S}^{*}(\{\otimes_{i\in I_{s}}^{!}L_{i}\}_{s\in S},M)\otimes\big(\otimes_{s\in S}{\textup{Lie}}(|I_{s}|)\big)\penalty\ .

with

P_{S}^{*}(\{\otimes_{i\in I_{s}}^{!}L_{i}\}_{s\in S},M)=\Hom_{\otimes_{s\in S}{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}\big(\otimes_{s\in S}(\otimes_{i\in I_{s}}^{!}L_{i}),M\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}[\lambda_{s}|s\in S]\big)\penalty\ .

Proposition 4.18

(
[
BDSHK19
]
)
.

A vertex Poisson algebra is a Lie algebra internal to the operad
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{c}
.

Proof.

The subspace of binary operations on a single colour is

P^{c}_{2}(\{L,L\},L)=\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}(L\otimes L,L[\lambda])\oplus\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]}(L\otimes^{!}L,L)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}(2)

and so the binary Lie bracket structure map internal to
P^{c}_{2}(\{L,L\},L)
decomposes as a direct sum of a commutative multiplication in
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{!}
and a Lie bracket internal to
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}^{*}
. Checking that the Jacobi relations imply the remaining conditions is the content of Theorem 10.7 of
[
BDSHK19
]
.
∎

4.3.
Chiral quantization

Before formalizing the notion of chiral quantization in the language of deformation theory, we recall a more hands on definition of chiral quantization and review a few examples. First, however, we need to introduce
\hbar
-adic vertex algebras.

Definition 4.19
.

A
\hbar
-adic (non-unital) vertex algebra is a pair
(V_{\hat{\hbar}},I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda})
where

(i)

V_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]{\textup{-Mod}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}{\textup{-Mod}})
, is such that the underlying
{\mathbb{K}}[\![\hbar]\!]
-module is complete in the
\hbar
-adic topology and trivializable,
i.e.
, there exists an isomorphism
V_{\hat{\hbar}}\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{0}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
, where
V_{0}=V\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}

(ii)

I_{{}_{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}
is an
\hbar
-adic integral
\lambda
-bracket
V_{\hat{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}V_{\hat{\hbar}}\rightarrow V_{\hat{\hbar}}[\lambda]
,

such that for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the truncations
(V/\hbar^{m},I_{\lambda}/\hbar^{m})
are vertex algebras over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
.

A
unital

\hbar
-adic vertex algebra is a
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra with a distinguished element
\ket{0}\in V_{\hat{\hbar}}
such that each truncation, modulo
\hbar^{m}
, is a unital vertex algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
.

Consider the action of the multiplicative group on
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
which scales
\hbar
with unit weight—we denote this distinguished multiplicative group by
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
.

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
V_{\hat{\hbar}}
be a
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra with an action of
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
by automorphisms. If the induced
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-grading on
V_{\hat{\hbar}}
is bounded below, the filtered
{\mathbb{K}}[\partial]
-module

{\rm Fil}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})\coloneqq\big(V_{\hat{\hbar}}\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\![\hbar]\!]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}(\!(\hbar)\!)\big)^{\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}}\penalty\ ,

is a vertex algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}
.

Proof.

Since the grading on
V_{\hat{\hbar}}
is bounded below,
{\rm Fil}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})
is a filtered
{\mathbb{K}}
-vector space,
i.e.
, it contains no infinite linear combinations. Thus, to show that
{\rm Fil}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})
is a vertex algebra all that remains is to show that the restriction of
I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}
to
{\rm Fil}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})
preserves it.

For any homogeneous
v,w\in V_{\hat{\hbar}}
, the coefficients of
\lambda
in
I_{\lambda}(v,w)
are polynomial in
\hbar
since the grading is bounded below. Thus, the restriction of
I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}
to
{\rm Fil}(V)
is an integral
\lambda
-bracket.
∎

Proposition 4.21
.

The filtered construction gives an equivalence of categories between graded
\hbar
-adic vertex algebras with bounded below grading and filtered vertex algebras over
{\mathbb{K}}
:

\displaystyle{\rm Vert}_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}^{\textup{gr},+}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{\rm Vert}_{{\mathbb{K}}}^{\rm fil}\penalty\ ,

\displaystyle V_{\hat{\hbar}}

\displaystyle\mapsto{\rm Fil}(V)

Proof.

This is essentially the Rees correspondence for filtered vertex algebras.
∎

A
\hbar
-adic unital vertex algebra
V_{\hat{\hbar}},I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}
is said to be
almost commutative
if the image of

I_{0,\lambda}:V_{0}\otimes V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0}[\lambda]

is contained in
V_{0}
. Then,
I_{0,\lambda}
actually defines a commutative product on
V/\hbar V
making it a unital commutative algebra with derivation.

Proposition 4.22
.

Suppose
(V_{\hat{\hbar}},I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda})
is an almost commutative, unital
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra. Then
V_{0}
is naturally a vertex Poisson algebra with commutative multiplication
I_{0,\lambda}
and
\lambda
-bracket

[\cdot_{\lambda}\cdot]=\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\bigg(\frac{1}{\hbar}\frac{d}{d\lambda}I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)\bigg)\penalty\ .

Definition 4.23
.

A
quantization
of a vertex Poisson algebra,
V
, is an almost commutative
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra
(V_{\hat{\hbar}},I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}
such that
V_{0}\cong V
as vertex Poisson algebras, where
V_{0}
possesses the vertex Poisson algebra structure from Proposition
4.22
.

A
chiral quantization
of a Poisson variety
Y
is a quantization of the vertex Poisson algebra
{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathcal{J}}Y]
.

4.3.1.
Current algebras

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a simple Lie algebra and let
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
be its linear dual, considered as Poisson variety with the Kirillov–Kostant–Souriau Poisson bracket. While
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
is not symplectic, it is smooth and so falls within the purview of our results. The functions on the arc space
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathcal{J}}\mathfrak{g}^{*})
, have the structure of a coisson algebra, with
\lambda
-bracket

[x_{\lambda}y]=[x,y]\penalty\ ,

for
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}\subset{\mathcal{O}}({\mathcal{J}}\mathfrak{g}^{*})
.

For
k\in{\mathbb{K}}
, we write
V^{k}(\mathfrak{g})
for the universal affine vertex algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. This is a vertex algebra strongly generated by
\mathfrak{g}\subset V^{k}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
with integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}(x,y)=\,:xy:+\lambda[x,y]+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{4h^{\vee}}\langle x,y\rangle\penalty\ ,

corresponding to the OPE

x(z)y(w)\sim\frac{k}{2h^{\vee}}\frac{\langle x,y\rangle}{(z-w)^{2}}+\frac{[x,y](w)}{z-w}\penalty\ ,

for
x,y\in\mathfrak{g}
,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
the Killing form, and
h^{\vee}
the dual Coxeter number. Similarly, let
V^{k}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
denote the homogenized universal affine vertex algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
at level
k
. This is a
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra strongly generated by the subspace
\mathfrak{g}\subset V^{k}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
with integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}(x,y)=\,:xy:+\lambda\hbar[x,y]+\frac{\lambda^{2}\hbar^{2}}{4h^{\vee}}\langle x,y\rangle\penalty\ ,

Proposition 4.24

(
[
AKM15
]
)
.

We have an isomorphism of vertex algebras over
{\mathbb{K}}
,

{\rm Fil}(V^{k}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g}))=\big(V^{k}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}{\mathbb{K}}(\!(\hbar)\!)\big)^{\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}}\cong V^{k}(\mathfrak{g})\penalty\ .

Proof.

This is a special case of Theorem 3.2.6.1 of
[
AKM15
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.25
.

The
\hbar
-adic vertex algebras
V_{\hbar}^{k}(\mathfrak{g})
give a one-parameter family of graded chiral quantizations of
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
.

Remark 4.26
.

Indeed, this is a special case of a much larger class of quantizations: given a vertex Lie algebra
L
, the homogenized chiral envelope
{\mathcal{V}}_{\hbar}(L)
is a quantization of the coisson algebra
\textup{Sym}(L)
, see
[
LI04
,
Cas23
]
for more details.

4.3.2.
Chiral differential operators

Let
Y
be a smooth affine variety with vanishing second Chern class
1
1

1

Chiral differential operators can actually be defined on more general smooth schemes with vanishing second Chern class but we shall only consider the affine case

. Let
\alpha\in\Omega^{3,cl}_{\rm dR}(M)
be a closed three-form viewed as a map

\alpha:\Theta(Y)\otimes\Theta(Y)\rightarrow\Omega^{1}(Y)\cong\partial{\mathcal{O}}(Y)\penalty\ ,

and extended to
\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y)
by
{{\partial}}
-linearity. Write
L_{Y}^{\alpha}
for the extension
2
2

2

In the language of
[
BD04
]
,
L^{\alpha}_{Y}
is a chiral
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid and is a chiral extension of the tangent
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid of
{\mathcal{J}}Y
. We shall sweep this detail under the rug.

0\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}({\mathcal{J}}Y)\hookrightarrow L^{\alpha}_{Y}\twoheadrightarrow\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y)\rightarrow 0

of the tangent vertex Lie algebroid,
\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y)
, of
{\mathcal{J}}Y
. The closed three form defines a
\lambda
-bracket on
L_{Y}^{\alpha}
via

(4.1)

\begin{split}[x_{\lambda}y]&=[x,y]-\alpha(x,y)\penalty\ ,\\
[x_{\lambda}a]&=x(a)\penalty\ ,\end{split}

for
x,y\in\Theta(Y)\subset\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y)
and
a\in{\mathcal{O}}(Y)\subset{\mathcal{O}}({\mathcal{J}}Y)
.

Definition 4.27
.

A vertex algebra of chiral differential operators (cdos),
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\alpha}(Y)
, is defined as the vertex envelope of
L_{Y}^{\alpha}
for some
\alpha\in\Omega^{c,cl}_{\rm dR}(Y)
.

Concretely
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\alpha}(Y)
is strongly generated by the subspace
L_{Y}^{\alpha}\subset{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\alpha}(Y)
with non-vanishing
\lambda
-brackets

I_{\lambda}(x,y)=[x,y]-\alpha(x,y)\penalty\ ,\text{ and }I_{\lambda}(x,a)=x(a)\penalty\ .

One can define the
\hbar
-adic analogue,
L^{\alpha}_{\hbar,Y}
, by homogenizing the
\lambda
-brackets

(4.2)

\begin{split}[x_{\lambda}y]&=\hbar[x,y]-\hbar^{2}c(x,y)\penalty\ ,\\
[x_{\lambda}a]&=\hbar x(a)\penalty\ .\end{split}

Note that
L_{\hbar,Y}^{\alpha}
is graded by
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
with
\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y)
having weight one and
{\mathcal{O}}({\mathcal{J}}Y)
having weight zero. Analogously, we define the
\hbar
-adic chiral differential operators
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\hbar,\alpha}
as the vertex envelope of
L_{\hbar,Y}^{\alpha}
.

Proposition 4.28
.

We have an isomorphism of vertex algebras over
{\mathbb{K}}

{\rm Fil}({\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\hbar,\alpha}(Y))\cong{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\alpha}(Y)

Proof.

Proven in Appendix A of
[
BN25
]
.
∎

Proposition 4.29
.

For every
\alpha\in\Omega^{3,cl}(Y)
,
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\hbar,\alpha}
is a graded chiral quantization of
T^{*}Y
.

Proof.

As a commutative algebra (with derivation) the classical limit of the vertex envelope is just
\textup{Sym}(\Theta({\mathcal{J}}Y))\cong{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathcal{J}}T^{*}Y]
. Furthermore, the
\hbar\rightarrow 0
limit of the
\lambda
-brackets of
L^{\alpha}_{\hbar,Y}
recovers the usual
\lambda
-bracket on
{\mathbb{K}}[{\mathcal{J}}T^{*}Y]
induced from the symplectic structure on
T^{*}Y
and thus
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\hbar,\alpha}
is a chiral quantization of
T^{*}Y
.

The grading on
L_{\hbar,Y}^{\alpha}
induces a
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-grading on
{\mathcal{D}}^{{\rm ch}}_{\hbar,\alpha}
making it a graded chiral quantization.
∎

4.3.3.
The Virasoro algebra and its minimal models

Let
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}
be the Virasoro vertex algebra at central charge
c\in{\mathbb{K}}
,
i.e.
, the algebra strongly generated by the field
T
with integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}(T,T)=\frac{1}{8}\lambda^{4}c+\lambda^{2}T+\lambda{{\partial}}T\penalty\ ,

corresponding to the OPE

T(z)T(w)\sim\frac{c/2}{(z-w)^{4}}+\frac{2T(w)}{(z-w)^{2}}+\frac{\partial T(w)}{z-w}\penalty\ .

It is well known that for certain values of the central charge,
c=c_{p,q}
, with
p,q
coprime and

c_{p,q}=1-6\frac{(p-q)^{2}}{pq}\penalty\ ,

the Virasoro algebra develops a vertex ideal such that the quotient is a rational vertex algebra known as the
(p,q)
minimal model. In particular, for
(p,q)=(2,2n+1)
, with
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the vertex ideal
{\mathcal{I}}_{n}\subset{\textup{Vir}}^{c}
is generated by a state of the form

T^{n}+\text{ states with conformal weight }2n\penalty\ .

We write,

{\mathcal{M}}_{n}\coloneqq{\textup{Vir}}^{c_{2,2n+1}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{n}\penalty\ ,

for the corresponding minimal model.

We define the homogenized
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}
as being generated by
T
with integral
\lambda
-bracket

I_{\lambda}(T,T)=\frac{1}{8}\hbar^{2}\lambda^{4}c+\lambda^{2}\hbar T+\lambda\hbar{{\partial}}T\penalty\ .

Clearly, the integral
\lambda
-bracket is invariant with respect to the
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-action which assigns unit weight to
T
. Indeed, this grading is related (via the Rees correspondence) to the, so-called,
R
-filtration of
[
AABLR26
]
.

For
c=c_{2,2n+1}
,
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}
develops a vertex ideal,
{\mathcal{I}}_{\hbar,n}
, generated by a state of the form
T^{n}+O(\hbar)
. We denote the quotient as

{\mathcal{M}}_{\hbar,n}\coloneqq{\textup{Vir}}^{c_{2.2n+1}}/{\mathcal{I}}_{\hbar,n}\penalty\ .

Lemma 4.30
.

We have isomorphisms of vertex algebras

(4.3)

\begin{split}\big({\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\![\hbar]\!]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}(\!(\hbar)\!)\big)^{\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}}&\cong{\textup{Vir}}^{c}\penalty\ ,\\
\big({\mathcal{M}}_{\hbar,n}\underset{{\mathbb{K}}[\![\hbar]\!]}{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}(\!(\hbar)\!)\big)^{\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}}&\cong{\mathcal{M}}_{n}\penalty\ .\end{split}

Proof.

Since
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{\hbar,n}
are positively graded by
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
, by Lemma
4.20
the invariants are a vertex algebra. We have a
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-equivariant embedding of
\hbar
-adic vertex algebras

(4.4)

\begin{split}{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}&\rightarrow{\textup{Vir}}^{c}[\![\hbar]\!]\\
T&\mapsto\hbar T\end{split}

which is an isomorphism after inverting
\hbar
. Since
\big({\textup{Vir}}^{c}(\!(\hbar)\!)\big)^{\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}}\cong{\textup{Vir}}^{c}
we have the first desired isomorphism. The result for the minimal model follows since the embedding maps
{\mathcal{I}}_{\hbar,n}
to
\hbar^{n}{\mathcal{I}}_{n}[\![\hbar]\!]
.
∎

We identify the classical limit of
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}
with
V={\mathcal{J}}{\mathbb{K}}[T]
with vertex Poisson algebra structure

[T_{\lambda}T]=2\lambda T+\partial T\penalty\ .

By construction,
{\textup{Vir}}^{c}_{\hbar}
is a graded quantization of
V
for any
c\in{\mathbb{K}}
.

Let
I_{n}\subset V
be the differential ideal generated by
T^{n}
and note that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\hbar,n}|_{\hbar=0}\cong I_{n}
. We write
M_{n}\coloneqq({\mathcal{J}}{\mathbb{K}}[T])/I_{n}\cong{\mathcal{J}}({\mathbb{K}}[T]/T^{n})
.

Lemma 4.31
.

The vertex algebra
{\mathcal{M}}_{\hbar,n}
is a graded quantization of the vertex Poisson algebra
M_{n}
.

Remark 4.32
.

Unlike the chiral differential operators and current algebras of the previous example the Virasoro algebra is not a chiral quantization of a Poisson algebra, though it is a quantization of a vertex Poisson algebra.

Moreover, the minimal models furnish a nice example of quantizations of vertex Poisson algebras, whose underlying commutative algebras are not smooth. This will lead to some challenges when we address their deformation theory in Section
9.5
.

Remark 4.33
.

Every
(p,q)
-minimal model has a Zhu’s
C_{2}
algebra of the form
{\mathbb{K}}[T]/T^{m}
for some
m
. However, the boundary minimal models of form
(2,2n+1)
are special in that they are classically free,
i.e.
, there is an isomorphism between the associated graded of the Li filtration and the arcs to the Zhu’s
C_{2}
algebra.

5.
The deformation-obstruction complex

For the remainder of the text, unless stated otherwise, all operads will be operads on a single colour, internal to the symmetric monoidal categories
\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}
or
\textup{DGVect}_{\mathbb{K}}
, which we will call plain or DG operads, respectively. We also identify plain operads with DG operads concentrated in cohomological degree zero, as usual.

5.1.
Koszul preliminaries

First, let us recall the notion of a single coloured co-operad, dual to the definition of an operad recalled in Remark
2.2
.

Definition 5.1
.

A single coloured co-operad is a counital, comonoid in the category
({{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}},\circ)
,
i.e.
, a tuple
({\mathcal{C}},\Delta,\eta)
where
{\mathcal{C}}\in{{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}}
,
\Delta:{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
is a coassociative comultiplication—called the decomposition—with counit
\eta:{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Let
E\in{{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}}
, similar to the free operad
{\mathcal{F}}(E)
, the free cooperad on
E
,
{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)
, is a cooperad structure on the same underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-module as
{\mathcal{F}}(E)
. We say that a cooperad
{\mathcal{C}}
is generated by
E
if there exist some subspace of corelations
R\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)
defining a cooperadic coideal,
(R)
, such that

{\mathcal{C}}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)/(R)\penalty\ .

The free operad admits a grading,
{\mathcal{F}}(E)=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{F}}(E)^{(n)}
, called the
weight grading
, characterized by setting

{\mathcal{F}}(E)^{(0)}=(0,{\mathbb{K}},0,\dots)\penalty\ ,\qquad{\mathcal{F}}(E)^{(1)}=E\subset{\mathcal{F}}(E)\penalty\ ,

and letting the weight be additive under composition. For a binary, free operad, the weight grading corresponds to the arity, up to a shift by one:
{\mathcal{F}}(E)^{(n)}={\mathcal{F}}(E)(n+1)
. Similarly,
{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{F}}^{c,(n)}(E)
has the same weight grading.

Definition 5.2
.

A quadratic operad,
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}(E,R)
is an operad that is presented as a quotient of the free operad,
{\mathcal{F}}(E)
, on
E
by a relation
R{\ \hookrightarrow\ }{\mathcal{F}}(E)
that factors as
R{\ \hookrightarrow\ }{\mathcal{F}}(E)^{(2)}
.

Similarly, a quadratic cooperad
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}(E,R)
is a cooperad presented as a suboperad of the free cooperad
{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)
by a corelation
R{\ \hookrightarrow\ }{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)
that factors as
R{\ \hookrightarrow\ }{\mathcal{F}}^{c,(2)}(E)\subset{\mathcal{F}}^{c}(E)
.

Since the (co)relations of a quadratic (co)operad are homogeneous of weight two, the weight grading descends to the quadratic (co)operad.

The operads
Comm
,
Lie
,
Ass
, and
Pois
that we recalled in Section
2.3
are all examples of quadratic operads—in fact they are all binary quadratic operads.

Quadratic operads are nice in that they enjoy a kind of Koszul duality:

Definition 5.3
.

The Koszul dual cooperad
\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
of a quadratic operad
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}(E,R)
is the DG cooperad

\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}=\mathcal{C}(E[1],R[2])\qquad\text{with}\qquad R[2]\to\mathcal{F}^{c}(E)^{(2)}[2]\cong\mathcal{F}^{c}(E[1])^{(2)}\penalty\ .

We finish by recalling the cobar construction. Let
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{C}}(E,R)
be a quadratic cooperad, then the cobar construction is the quasi-free DG operad

\Omega{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{F}}(\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}[-1],d_{\Omega{\mathcal{C}}})

on the coideal
\overline{{\mathcal{C}}}={\mathcal{C}}/{\mathcal{C}}^{(0)}
with differential
d_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{F}(\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1])\to\mathcal{F}(\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1])[1]
defined in terms of the decomposition
\Delta:{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\circ{\mathcal{C}}
as

d_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}|_{\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1]}:\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1]\xrightarrow{\Delta_{\mathcal{C}}[-1]}\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1]\circ\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1][1]\subset\mathcal{F}(\overline{\mathcal{C}}[-1])[1]\ .

For the special case where
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
for a quadratic operad
{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}(E,R)
, the cobar construction and Koszul duality are related by

Proposition 5.4
.

There is a natural projection of operads
\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\rightarrow({\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}})^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\cong{\mathcal{P}}
inducing an isomorphism

H^{0}(\Omega\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{P}\penalty\ ,

on cohomology.

Proof.

See the discussion preceding Proposition 7.3.2 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

One might hope that
\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
provides a quasi-free resolution of
{\mathcal{P}}
. Though this is not always the case, it is true for a special class of operads:

Definition 5.5
.

An operad
\mathcal{P}
is called
Koszul
if it is quadratic and the natural projection
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\to\mathcal{P}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Remark 5.6
.

The familiar operads
Comm
,
Lie
,
Ass
, and
Pois
are all Koszul.

5.2.
Maurer–Cartan elements

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a cooperad and
{\mathcal{Q}}
be an operad, then

\Hom_{{\mathfrak{S}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{Q}})=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}({\mathcal{C}}(n),{\mathcal{Q}}(n))\penalty\ ,

has a natural convolution product
\star:\Hom_{{\mathfrak{S}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{Q}})\otimes\Hom({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{Q}})\rightarrow\Hom_{{\mathfrak{S}}}({\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{Q}})
. In fact, this convolution product is naturally pre-Lie,
i.e.
, its commutator defines a Lie bracket

(5.1)

[\cdot,\cdot]:\Hom(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q})^{\otimes 2}\to\Hom(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q})\qquad[f,g]=f\star g-g\star f\ .

In summary:

Proposition 5.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a DG cooperad and
\mathcal{Q}
a DG operad. Then

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q}}=\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q})\penalty\ ,

is naturally a DG Lie algebra with respect to the convolution Lie bracket defined above, and the natural cohomological grading and differential induced by those on
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Proof.

See Proposition 6.4.5 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

In particular, if
{\mathcal{P}}
is a quadratic operad we write

\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}\coloneqq\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},{\mathcal{Q}}}=\Hom_{{\mathfrak{S}}}({\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},{\mathcal{Q}})\penalty\ ,

with cohomological grading,

\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(n)[1-n]\qquad\text{where}\qquad\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(n)=\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}({\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(n),{\mathcal{Q}}(n))\penalty\ .

5.2.1.
Algebra structures and Maurer–Cartan elements

Recall the weight grading on the quadratic operad
{\mathcal{P}}
. The DG Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
thus inherits an auxiliary grading, also called the
weight grading
, given by

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}=\bigoplus_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{(\ell)}\qquad\text{where}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{(\ell)}=\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}}({\mathcal{P}^{{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}}}^{(\ell)},\mathcal{Q})\ .

Definition 5.8
.

A Maurer–Cartan element, or twisting homomorphism,
\alpha\in\mathfrak{g}^{1}_{{\mathcal{C}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
is an element satisfying

d\alpha+\tfrac{1}{2}[\alpha,\alpha]=0\penalty\ .

We let
{{\textup{MC}}}_{\infty}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q}})\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C},\mathcal{Q}}^{1}
denote the subvariety of Maurer–Cartan elements. In particular, for the space of Maurer–Cartan elements of pure weight one, we drop the subscript,
i.e.
,

{{\textup{MC}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}})\coloneqq{{\textup{MC}}}_{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}})\cap\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(1)}\penalty\ .

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be a quadratic, Koszul, DG operad and
{\mathcal{Q}}
some arbitrary single coloured DG operad, then

{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{Q}})\cong{{\textup{MC}}}\big(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}\big)\subset\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}^{1,(1)}\penalty\ .

Proof.

Recall the cobar construction for
{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
,
\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
—since it is quasi-free

{\textup{Alg}}_{\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}}({\mathcal{Q}})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\textup{DG}}}(\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},{\mathcal{Q}})\cong\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}}_{\textup{DG}}}(\overline{{\mathcal{P}}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},{\mathcal{Q}})[1]\cong{{\textup{MC}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}})\penalty\ ,

where the first isomorphism is from the free-forgetful adjunction and the second isomorphism is precisely the content of Theorem
6.5.10
of
[
LV12
]
.

From Proposition 10.1.7 of
loc. cit.
, for a Koszul operad
{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{Q}})\hookrightarrow{\textup{Alg}}_{\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}}({\mathcal{Q}})
and in terms of Maurer-Cartan elements this corresponds to the subvariety of Maurer–Cartan elements that are of pure weight one,
i.e.
,

{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{Q}})\cong{{\textup{MC}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}})\subset\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}^{1,(1)}\penalty\ .

∎

Remark 5.10
.

The subvariety of all Maurer–Cartan elements
{{\textup{MC}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}})
corresponds to the space of
\Omega{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
structures, which are homotopy algebras.

5.2.2.
Graded Maurer–Cartan elements

Given two graded operads
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
, an algebra
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is called graded if it corresponds to a map of graded operads, that is, the underlying maps of
{\mathbb{K}}[\mathfrak{S}_{n}]
-modules respect the gradings at each arity. We let
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{gr}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
denote the space of graded
\mathcal{P}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}
.

Under the preceding hypotheses, the DG Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
inherits an auxiliary grading coming from those on
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
, which we denote by

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\langle j\rangle}\ ,

and with which the DG Lie algebra structure maps are naturally compatible, that is, the Lie bracket and differential have degree
0
. In this setting, we obtain the following identification:

Proposition 5.11
.

An element
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})={{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}})\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{1,(1)}
defines a graded algebra structure, that is,
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q})
, if and only if the corresponding Maurer–Cartan element is of degree
0
. Equivalently, the canonical identification of Proposition
5.9
defines an identification

{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\textup{DG}}^{\textup{gr}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\xrightarrow{\cong}{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{1,(1),\langle 0\rangle}\ .

Proof.

This follows from tracking the grading in the proof of Proposition
5.9
.

∎

5.2.3.
The twisted DG Lie algebra

Given
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})
viewed as a Maurer–Cartan element, the deformations of
\varphi
are controlled by an analogous DG Lie algebra, defined as follows:

Definition 5.12
.

The twisted DG Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
is defined by

{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}=\left(\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},\mathcal{Q})\ ,\ [\cdot,\cdot]\ ,\ d_{\varphi}\right)

where
[\cdot,\cdot]:\Hom(\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},\mathcal{Q})^{\otimes 2}\to\Hom(\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},\mathcal{Q})
is the bracket defined in Equation
5.1
and

(5.2)

d_{\varphi}:{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}[1]\qquad\text{is defined by}\qquad d_{\varphi}(\cdot)=d(\cdot)+[\varphi,\cdot]\ .

The twisted DG Lie algebra controls deformations of
\varphi
in the following sense.

Proposition 5.13
.

For any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})\cong{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})^{(1)}
, we have

\alpha\in{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})\qquad\text{if and only if}\qquad\varphi+\alpha\in{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})\ ,

and the resulting deformed DG Lie algebras are naturally equal, that is, we have
({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{\alpha}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi+\alpha}
.

Proof.

These follow from straightforward calculations; the former is Proposition 12.2.4 of
[
LV12
]
.
∎

Iterating the preceding Proposition, we deduce:

Corollary 5.14
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})\cong{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})
and
\alpha\in{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})
. Then we have

\beta\in{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi+\alpha})\qquad\text{if and only if}\qquad\alpha+\beta\in{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})\ .

In other words, further deformations of the deformed algebra
\varphi+\alpha
are equivalent to deformations of the original algebra. Equivalently, given deformations
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
of
\varphi
, we obtain that
\beta=\alpha_{2}-\alpha_{1}
a deformation of
\varphi+\alpha_{1}
to
\varphi+\alpha_{2}
, and conversely.

We finish by defining the operadic deformation-obstruction complex of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})
, a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}
:

We now introduce the operadic deformation-obstruction complex of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{Q}})
, a
{\mathcal{P}}
-algebra internal to
{\mathcal{Q}}
. As we shall see in
6.1
, this complex controls the deformation theory of
\varphi
.

Definition 5.15
.

The operadic deformation-obstruction complex of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})
is the cochain complex

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=({\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\ ,\ d_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad d_{\varphi}:{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}[1]

is the differential defined in Equation
5.2
, and denote by
Z^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
and
H^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
the subcomplex of cocycles and the cohomology of
C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
, respectively.

Given
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q})
, the auxiliary grading is also compatible with the DG Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi}
, since the modified differential
d_{\varphi}
is still of degree
0
with respect to the auxiliary grading, as the Maurer–Cartan element corresponding to
\varphi
is itself of degree
0
. In particular, the deformation complex inherits a grading, which is preserved by the differential and thus induces a grading on cohomology,

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}C^{\bullet,\langle j\rangle}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)\qquad\text{and}\qquad H^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}H^{\bullet,\langle j\rangle}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)\penalty\ .

5.3.
Deformation-obstruction complex for algebras of the classical kind

Throughout this section we fix a vector space
A\in\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}
and set
{\mathcal{Q}}={\textup{End}}_{\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}}(A)
to be the endomorphism operad of
A
.

5.3.1.
Associative algebras

Proposition 5.16
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Ass}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}}(A)
is given by the usual complex of (reduced) Hochschild cochains, that is,

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Hoc}}(A,m_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Hoc}}(A,m_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{Hoch}}\right)\ ,

for
m_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
the associative algebra structure map and
d_{\text{Hoch}}:C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Hoc}}(A,m_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Hoc}}(A,m_{\varphi})[1]
the usual Hochschild differential determined by
m_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that at each arity
n
, we have

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(n)=\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]{\textup{-Mod}}}({\textup{Ass}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(n),{\textup{End}}(V)(n))\cong{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}\Hom(V^{\otimes n},V)\cong\Hom(V^{\otimes n},V)

and that the differential
d_{\varphi}=[\varphi,\cdot]
given by commutator against the Maurer-Cartan element corresponding to the associative algebra structure map is equivalent to the usual Hochschild formula of cyclic pre- and post-composition, under the infinitesimal composition maps, with the multiplication structure map
m_{\varphi}
, which is precisely the usual Hochschild differential.
∎

5.3.2.
Commutative algebras

Recall from
2.3.3
that there is a canonical map
{\textup{Ass}}\to{\textup{Comm}}
, the pullback along which corresponds to the natural inclusion of commutative into associative algebras
{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Comm}}(\mathcal{Q})\to{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Ass}}(\mathcal{Q})
. This induces a map on Koszul dual cooperads

{\textup{Ass}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\to{\textup{Comm}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\penalty\ .

In fact, this implies the following:

Proposition 5.17
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Comm}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is given by the usual complex of (reduced) Harrison cochains, that is,

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(A,m_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(A,m_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{Har}}\right)\ ,

where
\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}\subset\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)
denotes the subspace of
n
-ary multilinear maps which vanish on sums over
(p,q)
-shuffles for some fixed
p+q=n
of the correspondingly permuted tensor products
, that is,

\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}=\{m\in\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)\ |\sum_{\sigma\in{\textup{Sh}}_{p,q}}m(v_{\sigma^{-1}(1)}\otimes\dots\otimes v_{\sigma^{-1}(p+q)})=0\text{ for all }v\in A^{\otimes p},\ p+q=n\}\penalty\ .

Also
m_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
denotes the generating structure map of the commutative algebra, and
d_{\text{Har}}
is the usual Harrison differential.

Proof.

This is essentially Proposition 13.1.4 of
[
LV12
]
.
∎

We now describe an equivalent model for the deformation theory of commutative algebras, called André–Quillen cohomology, and a generalization thereof which will be relevant for describing the deformation theory of Poisson algebras below.

Viewing
A
as a commutative
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra with commutative multiplication
m_{\varphi}
, we write
\Omega^{1}_{A}\in A{\textup{-Mod}}
for the module of Kähler differentials. Given a cofibrant resolution
\tilde{A}\to A
, the cotangent complex
{\mathbb{L}}_{A}\in{\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})
is defined as the image of the object
\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}\in{\textup{D}}(\tilde{A}{\textup{-Mod}})
under the equivalence
{\textup{D}}(\tilde{A}{\textup{-Mod}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})
. Similarly, we define
{\mathbb{L}}^{p}_{A}\in{\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})
as the image of the object
\Omega^{p}_{\tilde{A}}=\bigwedge_{\tilde{A}}^{p}\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{A}}\in{\textup{D}}(\tilde{A}{\textup{-Mod}})
under the same equivalence.

Definition 5.18
.

The André–Quillen cochains on
A
are defined by

C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})}({\mathbb{L}}_{A},A)\ .

Similarly, the degree-
p
higher André–Quillen cochains on
A
are defined by

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})}({\mathbb{L}}^{\wedge_{A}p+1}_{A},A)\ .

Note that our conventions are shifted by one from the more standard conventions of
[
Lod98
]
, so that for
p=0
we have
C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)=C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)
. Moreover, in this case the relevant cohomology groups are equivalent to the Harrison cohomology defined above:

Theorem 5.19
.

There is a natural isomorphism

H^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(A,m_{\varphi})\ .

Proof.

This is a special case of Theorem 12.4.3 of
[
LV12
]
.
∎

Furthermore, we have the following standard vanishing results for smooth algebras:

Theorem 5.20
.

Suppose
A
is smooth over
{\mathbb{K}}
. Then
{\mathbb{L}}_{A}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega_{A}^{1}\in A{\textup{-Mod}}={\textup{D}}(A{\textup{-Mod}})^{\heartsuit}
, and the object
\Omega_{A}^{1}
is projective.

Proof.

This is a standard result in algebraic geometry; see
[
Sta26
]
tags 08R4, 08RB, and 00T1.
∎

Corollary 5.21
.

Suppose
A
is smooth over
{\mathbb{K}}
. Then
C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)
is acyclic away from degree zero and

H^{0}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)\cong\Hom_{A{\textup{-Mod}}}(\Omega^{1}_{A},A)={\textup{Der}}(A,A)=:\Theta_{A}

the module of derivations
{\textup{Der}}(A,A)
of
A
, or tangent module
\Theta_{A}
of
A
.

More generally, for each
p\geq 1
,
C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)
is acyclic in non-zero degree, and

H^{0,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)\cong\Hom_{A{\textup{-Mod}}}(\Omega^{p+1}_{A},A)\cong\wedge^{p+1}_{A}\Theta_{A}\ ,

the module of degree
p+1
polyvector fields
\wedge^{p+1}_{A}\Theta_{A}
.

5.3.3.
Lie algebras

Proposition 5.22
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{\mathcal{C}}(A)
is given by the usual complex of (reduced) Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains, that is,

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(A,b_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(A,b_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{CE}}\right)\ ,

where
\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}={\mathbb{K}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}\Hom(A^{\otimes n},A)
denotes the
S_{n}
-coinvariants,
b_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
the Lie algebra structure map and
d_{\text{CE}}:C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(A,m_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(A,m_{\varphi})[1]
the usual Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
b_{\varphi}
.

Proof.

This is Proposition 13.2.2 of
[
LV12
]
.

∎

5.3.4.
Poisson algebras

Definition 5.23
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Pois}}({\textup{End}}(A))
a Poisson algebra structure on
A
with corresponding commutative product
m_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
and Lie bracket
b_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
. We define the operadic Poisson cochains on
(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})
by

C_{\textup{Pois}}^{\bullet}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=C_{{\textup{Pois}},{\textup{End}}(A)}^{\bullet}(\varphi)\ .

5.3.5.
A bicomplex structure on Poisson cochains

Recall that the Poisson operad has an auxiliary grading
{\textup{Pois}}=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}{{\textup{Pois}}}^{\langle m\rangle}
, with the bracket operation having weight one. This induces a grading on the Koszul dual cooperad

{\textup{Pois}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}=\bigoplus({\textup{Pois}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}})^{-p}\qquad\text{where}\qquad({\textup{Pois}}^{{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}})^{-p}={\textup{Lie}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(p+1)\circ{\textup{Comm}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}\penalty\ ,

in turn inducing an auxiliary grading on the Poisson cochains
\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n-1}\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(n)^{\langle p\rangle}[1-n]
where

\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(n)^{\langle p\rangle}=\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]{\textup{-Mod}}}\big(({\textup{Pois}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}})(n)^{\langle-p\rangle},{\textup{End}}_{\Vect_{{\mathbb{K}}}}(A)(n)\big)\penalty\ .

With respect to this auxiliary grading
d_{m_{\varphi}}
has degree zero but
d_{b_{\varphi}}
has degree one, and in fact we may identify this auxiliary grading with the column number of a bicomplex:

Proposition 5.24
.

There is a natural bicomplex
C^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\textup{Pois}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=\oplus_{p,q=0}^{\infty}C^{p,q}_{\textup{Pois}}
defined by

C^{p,q}_{\textup{Pois}}=C^{p,q}_{\textup{Pois}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(p+q+1)^{\langle p\rangle}\qquad

with horizontal and vertical differentials given, respectively, by

d_{h}=d_{b_{\varphi}}:C^{p,q}_{\textup{Pois}}\to C^{p+1,q}_{\textup{Pois}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad d_{v}=d_{m_{\varphi}}:C^{p,q}_{\textup{Pois}}\to C^{p,q+1}_{\textup{Pois}}\penalty\ ,

such that the total complex is naturally isomorphic to the operadic Poisson cochains, that is,

\textup{Tot }C^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\textup{Pois}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C_{\textup{Pois}}^{\bullet}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\ .

We can depict the bicomplex explicitly as follows, such that the graded degree corresponds to column number:

The zeroth row and column define quotient complexes

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(A,m_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(n)^{\langle 0\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m_{\varphi}})\qquad\text{and}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Pois}},b}(A,b_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(n)^{\langle n-1\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{b_{\varphi}})

which control the induced deformation of commutative algebra and Lie algebra, respectively, coming from a deformation of Poisson algebra, as the following Proposition confirms:

Proposition 5.25
.

There are natural identifications of complexes

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(A,m_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(A,m_{\varphi})\qquad\text{and}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Pois}},b}(A,b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(A,b_{\varphi})\ .

Proof.

This follows straightforwardly from the preceding discussion by unwinding the definitions.

∎

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
the
p^{th}
row of the above bicomplex defines a complex

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(V,m_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(n)^{\langle p\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m_{\varphi}})

and we have the following generalization of the preceding result:

Proposition 5.26
.

For each
p\geq 0
, there are natural identifications of complexes

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(A,m_{\varphi})[p]\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(A)\ ,

for
A=(A,m_{\varphi})
. In particular, we have
H^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(A,m_{\varphi})\cong\Hom_{A{\textup{-Mod}}}(\Omega^{p+1}_{A},A)
.

Proof.

This follows from Proposition 4.5.13 of
[
Lod98
]
, together with Proposition
7.1
below.
∎

5.3.6.

The bicomplex decomposition of Equation
5.3
is a manifestation of the fact that a deformation of a Poisson algebra is equivalent to a deformation of the underlying commutative algebra and Lie algebra in a way that is compatible with the Poisson algebra relations. A common variant that is often considered in the geometric context is the deformations of Poisson structure on a fixed underlying variety, or equivalently in the affine setting the deformations of the underlying Lie bracket which are compatible with the fixed commutative algebra structure. Such deformations are controlled by the following subcomplex:

Definition 5.27
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Pois}}({\textup{End}}(A))
a Poisson algebra stucture on
A
with corresponding commutative product
m_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
and Lie bracket
b_{\varphi}:A^{\otimes 2}\to A
. The (reduced) geometric Poisson cochains on
(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})
is the subcomplex of
C_{\textup{Pois}}^{\bullet}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})
given by

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}H^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{{\textup{Pois}},m}(A,m_{\varphi})[-p]\ ,\ d_{b_{\varphi}}\ \right)\ .

The following geometric description follows from Proposition
5.26
:

Corollary 5.28
.

The complex of geometric Poisson cochains admits an isomorphism of complexes

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\cong\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}\Hom_{A{\textup{-Mod}}}(\Omega^{p+1}_{A},A)[-p]\ ,\ \tilde{d}_{b_{\varphi}}\ \right)\ ,

where
\tilde{d}_{b}
is determined uniquely by the fact that the following diagram commutes

(5.4)
