Finally, by Corollary
5.21
, we obtain the following:

Corollary 5.29
.

Suppose the underlying commutative algebra
(A,m_{\varphi})
is smooth over
{\mathbb{K}}
. Then the inclusion of the subcomplex of geometric Poisson cochains is a quasi-isomorphism,

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C_{\textup{Pois}}^{\bullet}(A,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\ .

5.4.
Deformation-obstruction complex for algebras of the chiral kind

5.4.1.
Chiral algebras

In this section, we take
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
the Lie operad over
{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{\rm ch}}(R)
the endomorphism of operad of a D-module
R\in\mathcal{C}={D}(X)^{\rm ch}
with respect to the chiral pseudo tensor structure, so that the space
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}
is equal to the space of chiral algebra structures on the D-module
M
.

Proposition 5.30
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{\rm ch}}(R)
is given by the complex of (reduced) chiral cochains, that is,

\hskip-28.45274ptC^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{{\rm ch}}(R,\mu_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{{\rm ch}}(R,\mu_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{\rm ch}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{CE}}\right)\ ,

for
\mu_{\varphi}:j_{*}j^{*}(R^{\boxtimes 2})\to R
the chiral Lie bracket structure map and
d_{\text{CE}}:C^{\bullet}_{{\rm ch}}(R,\mu_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{{\rm ch}}(R,\mu_{\varphi})[1]
the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
\mu_{\varphi}
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{\rm ch}
.

Proof.

The is a specialization of Proposition
5.22
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{\rm ch}
.
∎

5.4.2.
Commutative chiral algebras

In this section, we take
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Comm}}_{\mathbb{K}}
the commutative operad over
{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{!}}(R)
the endomorphism of operad of a D-module
R\in\mathcal{C}={D}(X)^{\rm ch}
with respect to the
!
monoidal structure, so that the space
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}
is equal to the space of
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra structures on the D-module
R
.

Proposition 5.31
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Comm}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{!}}(R)
is given by the complex of (reduced) chiral Harrison cochains, that is,

\hskip-28.45274ptC^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(R,m_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(R,m_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{!}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{Har}}\right)\ ,

for
m_{\varphi}:R^{\otimes 2}\to R
is the
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
structure map and
d_{\text{Har}}:C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(R,m_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(R,m_{\varphi})[1]
the Harrison differential determined by
m_{\varphi}
, internal to the monoidal category
{D}(X)^{!}
.

Proof.

The proof is precisely the same as that of Proposition
5.17
, internal to the monoidal category
{D}(X)^{!}
,
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

We now describe the analogue of (higher) André–Quillen cohomology in the chiral setting. Consider

R
as
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
-algebra with the multiplication
m_{\varphi}
and let
\Omega^{1}_{R}\in{D}(R)
be the module of Kähler differentials, as defined in the discussion following Definition
3.21
. Given a cofibrant resolution
\tilde{R}\to R
of commutative algebras internal to
{\textup{D}}(X)^{!}
, the cotangent complex
{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\in{\textup{D}}(R)
is defined as the image of the object
\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{R}}\in{\textup{D}}(\tilde{R})
under the equivalence
{\textup{D}}(\tilde{R})\xrightarrow{\cong}{\textup{D}}(R)
. Similarly, we define
{\mathbb{L}}^{p}_{R}\in{\textup{D}}(R)
as the image of the object
\Omega^{p}_{\tilde{R}}=(\Omega^{1}_{\tilde{R}})^{\wedge_{\tilde{R}}p}\in{\textup{D}}(\tilde{R})
under the same equivalence.

Definition 5.32
.

The chiral André–Quillen cochains on
R
are defined by

C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(R)}({\mathbb{L}}_{R},R)\ .

Similarly, the degree-
p
higher André–Quillen cochains on
R
are defined by

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}}(\{{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1};R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\ ,

where
{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}
denotes the derived category of
R
-modules internal to
{\textup{D}}(X)^{!}
, equipped with the
*
pseudo-tensor structure, recalled in Definition
3.18
and Proposition
3.19
.

Note that for
p=0
we have
C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)=C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)
. Moreover, in this case the relevant cohomology groups are equivalent to the chiral Harrison cohomology defined above:

Theorem 5.33
.

There is a natural isomorphism

H^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(R,m_{\varphi})\ .

Proof.

This is again a special case of Theorem 12.4.3 of
[
LV12
]
, internal to the
{\mathbb{K}}
-linear monoidal category
{D}(X)^{!}
.
∎

Finally, we formulate the analogue of the vanishing of higher Andre-Quillen cohomology for smooth algebras in the chiral setting: we will use the notion of very smooth
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra, as in Definition 2.3.15 of
[
BD04
]
, which guarantees the following analogue of Theorem
5.20
in the chiral setting:

Proposition 5.34
.

Let
R
be a very smooth
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra. Then
{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega_{R}^{1}\in{D}(R)={\textup{D}}(R)^{\heartsuit}
, and the object
\Omega_{R}^{1}
is projective.

Proof.

This follows essentially by definition.
∎

The primary source of examples of very smooth
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebras used in the present work comes from the following:

Proposition 5.35
.

Let
B
be a smooth algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}
and
R=\mathcal{J}(B)
be the
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra of jets to
B
, as defined preceding Equation
3.2
. Then
R
is very smooth.

Proof.

This is explained in 2.3.17 of
[
BD04
]
.
∎

Corollary 5.36
.

Let
R
be a very smooth
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra. Then
C^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)
is acyclic in non-zero degree, and

H^{0}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\cong\Hom_{{D}(R)}(\Omega^{1}_{R},R)={\textup{Der}}(R,R)=h(\Theta_{R})

the module of derivations
{\textup{Der}}(R,R)
of
R
, as defined in Definition
3.21
, or equivalently the middle de Rham cohomology of the tangent module
\Theta_{R}\in{D}(R)
.

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
,
C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)
is acyclic except in cohomological degree
p
, and

H^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\cong P^{*}_{R,p+1}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\cong h(\wedge^{p+1}_{R}\Theta_{R})\ ,

the middle de Rham cohomology of the module of degree
{p+1}
polyvector fields
\wedge^{p+1}_{R}\Theta_{R}\in{D}(R)
.

5.4.3.
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras

Throughout this section, we take
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{P}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{*}}(R)
the endomorphism of operad of a D-module
R\in\mathcal{C}={D}(X)^{*}
with respect to the * pseudo-tensor structure, so that the space
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}
is equal to the space of
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra structures on the D-module
R
.

Proposition 5.37
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(R)
is given by the complex of (reduced) Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains in
{D}(X)^{*}
, that is,

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}}}(R,b_{\varphi})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{CE}(R,b_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{*}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\text{CE}}\right)\ ,

for
d_{\textup{CE}}:C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}}}(R,b_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}}}(R,b_{\varphi})[1]
the usual Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
b_{\varphi}
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{*}
.

Proof.

This is a specialization of Proposition
5.22
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{*}
.
∎

5.4.4.
Coisson algebras

In this section, we take
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{P}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(R)
the endomorphism of operad of a D-module
R\in\mathcal{C}={D}(X)^{c}
with respect to the classical pseudo-tensor structure, so that the space
{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}
is equal to the space of coisson algebra structures on the D-module
R
.

Proposition 5.38
.

The deformation-obstruction complex of Definition
5.15
for
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}
and
\mathcal{Q}={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(R)
is given by the complex of (reduced) Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains in
{D}(X)^{c}
, that is,

\hskip-28.45274ptC^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)=C^{\bullet}_{c}(R,\mu_{\varphi}^{c})\qquad\text{where}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{c}(R,\mu_{\varphi}^{c})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\text{CE}}\right)\ ,

for
\mu_{\varphi}^{c}=(m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\in P_{2}^{c}(\{R,R\},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{2}}
the classical Lie bracket structure map corresponding to a Coisson structure under Proposition
3.33
above, and
d_{\text{CE}}:C^{\bullet}_{c}(R,\mu_{\varphi})\to C^{\bullet}_{c}(R,\mu_{\varphi})[1]
the usual Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
\mu_{\varphi}
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{c}
.

Proof.

This is a specialization of
5.22
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{c}
.
∎

As we will explain, there is a bicomplex structure underlying the complex of classical cochains, analogous to that of Proposition
5.24
in the Poisson case.

Proposition 5.39
.

There is a natural bicomplex
C^{\bullet,\bullet}_{c}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=\oplus_{p,q=0}^{\infty}C^{p,q}_{c}
defined by

C^{p,q}_{c}\equiv C^{p,q}_{c}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=P_{p+q+1}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{p+q+1},R)_{S_{p+q+1}}^{\langle p\rangle}

with horizontal and vertical differentials given, respectively, by

d_{h}=d_{b_{\varphi}}:C^{p,q}_{c}\to C^{p+1,q}_{c}\qquad d_{v}=d_{m_{\varphi}}:C^{p,q}_{c}\to C^{p,q+1}_{c}

such that the total complex is naturally isomorphic to the classical cochains, that is,

\textup{Tot }C^{\bullet,\bullet}_{c}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C_{c}^{\bullet}(R,\mu_{\varphi}^{c})\ .

Proof.

The Lie operad is generated by a single binary operation, which thus uniquely determines the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential. The decomposition of the space of binary operations in
P^{c}
given in Equation
3.5
induces the decomposition of the differential as
d_{{\textup{CE}}}=d_{\mu_{\varphi}}+d_{b_{\varphi}}
and these components respect the bigrading essentially by definition, and—by the coisson algebra relations—they commute appropriately.
∎

The zeroth row and column define quotient complexes

C^{\bullet}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}^{\langle 0\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m_{\varphi}})\penalty\ ,

and

C^{\bullet}_{c,b}(R,b_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}^{\langle n-1\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{b_{\varphi}})\penalty\ ,

which control the induced deformation of
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
and
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra, respectively, coming from a deformation of Coisson algebra, as the following Proposition confirms:

Proposition 5.40
.

There are natural identifications of complexes

C^{\bullet}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(R,m_{\varphi})\qquad\text{and}\qquad C^{\bullet}_{c,b}(R,b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(R,b_{\varphi})\ .

Proof.

This follows straightforwardly from the preceding discussion by unwinding the definitions.
∎

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
the
p^{th}
column of the above bicomplex defines a complex

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})=(\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}P_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{S_{n}}^{\langle p\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m_{\varphi}})

and we have the following analogue of Proposition
5.26
:

Proposition 5.41
.

For each
p\geq 0
, there are natural identifications of complexes

C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})[p]\xrightarrow{\cong}C^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\ .

In particular, we have
H^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})\cong P_{R,p+1}^{*}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}
.

Proof.

The proof is given in detail for the corresponding statement about internal Hom objects in Proposition
8.32
, and the same proof applies here with fewer complications,
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

Remark 5.42
.

This is proved in Theorem 7.2 of
[
BDSH+21
]
for
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
in the translation invariant setting

5.4.5.

The analogue of the geometric Poisson cochains of Definition
5.27
, controlling deformations of a Poisson structure on a fixed underlying variety, is given by the following subcomplex:

Definition 5.43
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Lie}}({\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(R))
be a Coisson algebra
structure on
R
with corresponding commutative product
m_{\varphi}\in P_{2}^{!}(\{R,R\};R)
and Lie bracket
b_{\varphi}\in P_{2}^{*}(\{R,R\},R)
. The (reduced) variational Poisson cochains on
(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})
is the subcomplex of
C_{c}^{\bullet}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})
given by

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})=\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}H^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})[-p]\ ,\ d_{b_{\varphi}}\ \right)\ .

The following concrete description, analogous to Corollary
5.28
, follows from Proposition
5.41
:

Corollary 5.44
.

The complex of variational Poisson cochains admits an isomorphism of complexes

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\cong\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}P^{*}_{R,p+1}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}[-p]\ ,\ \tilde{d}_{b_{\varphi}}\ \right)\ ,

where
\tilde{d}_{b}
is determined uniquely by the fact that the following diagram commutes

(5.5)

Finally, by Corollary
5.36
, we obtain the following:

Corollary 5.45
.

Suppose the underlying
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra
(R,m_{\varphi})
is very smooth. Then the inclusion of the subcomplex of variational Poisson cochains is a quasi-isomorphism,

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\xrightarrow{\cong}C_{c}^{\bullet}(R,m_{\varphi},b_{\varphi})\ .

We remark that this was proved for
R
an algebra of differential polynomials in
[
BDSH+21
]
, that is, the algebra of jets to an affine space
\mathbb{A}^{d}
. The preceding corollary implies that this generalizes to the algebra of jets on a smooth
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra.

6.
Deformation theory

Throughout, let
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}={\textup{Spec\ }}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
and let
\mathbb{G}_{m}
act on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
by scaling
\hbar
with unit weight. We write
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
for this distinguished multiplicative group action.

6.1.
Deformation theory for algebras

Let
{\mathcal{P}}
be a binary, Koszul operad over
{\mathbb{K}}
and let
{\mathcal{Q}}
be a single coloured operad over
{\mathbb{K}}
. We now formalize the moduli problem controlling formal deformations of a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra structure internal to
\mathcal{Q}
.

Let
(S,\mathfrak{m}_{S})
be a local Artinian
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra with a closed embedding of
{\mathbb{K}}
-schemes
{\textup{Spec\ }}S\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
such that the closed point in
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
is mapped to the origin of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. We write
{\mathcal{P}}_{S}={\mathcal{P}}\otimes S
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{S}={\mathcal{Q}}\otimes S
for the constant families of
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}
over
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
.

Proposition 6.1
.

There is an isomorphism of DG Lie algebras

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}S

where the latter is equipped with the trivial
S
-linear extension of the differential
d:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}[1]
and Lie bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
.

Proof.

This is Lemma 12.2.5 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

There is a natural map of DG Lie algebras given by the evaluation at
\mathfrak{m}_{S}

(\cdot)/\mathfrak{m}_{S}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}}\otimes_{S}S/\mathfrak{m}_{S}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\

and thus a map on Maurer–Cartan solutions

{{\textup{MC}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{S},{\mathcal{Q}}_{S}})\rightarrow{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}})\penalty\ ,

taking an
S
-family of
{\mathcal{P}}
algebras, internal to
{\mathcal{Q}}
, to its restriction at the closed point
\mathfrak{m}_{S}
.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})\cong{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}
considered as a Maurer–Cartan element. The space of deformations of
\varphi
over
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
is

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)={{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\ .

Given some
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{Q}})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{\mathbb{K}}}({\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}})
, we can view this as a map between the fibres of
{\mathcal{P}}_{S}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{S}
at the closed point
\mathfrak{m}_{S}
. A deformation
\varphi_{S}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)
, therefore, can be understood to be an extension of this map to the whole
S
-family, such that the restriction to the central fibres agrees with
\varphi
.

The variety of deformations has a concrete realisation in terms of Maurer–Cartan elements:

Proposition 6.3
.

We have an isomorphism of varieties,

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)\cong{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathfrak{m}_{S})\ ,

where
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{S}
is the DG Lie algebra with the trivial
\mathfrak{m}_{S}
-linear extension of the differential and Lie bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}
.

Proof.

This is Proposition 12.2.6 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

6.1.1.
The stack of formal deformations

The weight
0
component of the Koszul dual co-operad
{\mathcal{P}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
is simply a copy of the base field
{\mathbb{K}}
in arity
1
,
i.e.
,

{\mathcal{P}}^{{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},(0)}=(0,{\mathbb{K}},0,0\dots)\qquad\text{and so}\qquad\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(0)}={\mathcal{Q}}(1)

For any single coloured (DG) operad
{\mathcal{Q}}
, the space of unary operations
{\mathcal{Q}}(1)
is a unital (DG) associative
{\mathbb{K}}
-algebra. Moreover, in the DG setting, the cohomological degree zero component,
{\mathcal{Q}}(1)^{0}
is an associative subalgebra of
{\mathcal{Q}}(1)
. For example if
{\mathcal{Q}}
is the endomorphism operad on the single colour
V\in\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}
, then
{\mathcal{Q}}(1)={\textup{End}}(V)
is just the endomorphism algebra of
V
.

Two deformations
\varphi_{S},\widetilde{\varphi}_{S}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)
of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{Q}})
are called equivalent if there is an invertible element
g\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{S}(1)^{0})^{\times}
such that
\varphi_{S}=g\widetilde{\varphi}g^{-1}
and
g/\mathfrak{m}_{S}=\mathbbm{1}\in{\mathcal{Q}}(1)^{0}
.

Definition 6.4
.

We define the stacks of formal deformations
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}
by

(6.1)

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)={\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)/\sim

where the right hand side denotes the quotient groupoid of
{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}(S)
by the equivalence relation defined above.

6.1.2.
The Maurer–Cartan stack

The weight zero subspace
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{(0)}
is naturally a Lie subalgebra and thus acts, via the adjoint action, on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
preserving the weight grading. Similarly, the weight and cohomological degree zero subspace
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{0,(0)}\subset\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}^{(0)}
is a further Lie subalgebra which acts on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}
preserving the weight and cohomological grading.

For a local Artinian ring
(S,\mathfrak{m}_{S})
as above, one can define the analogous subalgebras

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{0,(0)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}\mathfrak{m}_{S}\ \subset\ {\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{0}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}\mathfrak{m}_{S}\ \subset\ {\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}\mathfrak{m}_{S}\penalty\ .

Since these subalgebras are all nilpotent, we have formal exponential algebraic groups

\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0}=\exp(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathfrak{G}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}=\exp(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)})\penalty\ .

These groups act on the space of solutions to the Maurer–Cartan equations, so that we have the following definition:

Definition 6.5
.

The Maurer–Cartan stack
{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\varphi}
is defined as

(6.2)

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\varphi}(S)=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathfrak{m}_{S})^{(1)}/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}\right]\ .

This action encodes the automorphisms of the corresponding
\mathcal{P}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}
in the sense of the following proposition.

Proposition 6.6
.

There are isomorphisms of algebraic stacks

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\varphi}\xrightarrow{\cong}{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}\ .

Proof.

This is Theorem 12.2.10 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

Remark 6.7
.

We also record for later reference the following formally equivalent description of the Maurer–Cartan stack, which will be more amenable to generalization later, in particular in Definition
6.11
:

\displaystyle{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\varphi}(S)

\displaystyle=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\mathfrak{m}_{S})/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}\right]

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}\left[\left({{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}})\times_{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})}\{\varphi\}\right)/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}\right]

(6.3)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\cong}\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}})/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}\right]\times_{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})}\{\varphi\}

where the first isomorphism follows from Proposition
6.3
, and the second since the group acts trivially on
{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})
and
\{\varphi\}
, so that in particular the restriction map

\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{S},\mathcal{Q}_{S}})/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}^{0,(0)}\right]\to{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}})

is well-defined.

6.1.3.

Recall the deformation-obstruction complex of
5.15
, this complex controls the deformation theory of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}}({\mathcal{Q}})
in the sense of the following Proposition:

Proposition 6.8
.

We have an isomorphism of varieties and stacks, respectively,

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{2})\cong Z^{1}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(\varphi)\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{2})\cong H^{1}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(\varphi)\penalty\ .

Proof.

This is precisely Theorem 12.2.7 in
[
LV12
]
.
∎

At higher order in
\hbar
, the existence of a deformation is, in general, obstructed. Nevertheless, the space of such order-by-order deformations remains a torsor for
H^{1}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}(\varphi)
. What follows is a strengthening of Theorems 12.2.8 and 12.2.9 of
[
LV12
]
, following similar proofs to those of
loc. cit.

Anticipating the following proposition, we define the obstruction cochain associated with
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
by

{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-k}]\ \in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{2,(2)}\ .

Then we have:

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})
and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
. Then the obstruction associated to
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
is closed, i.e.,

d_{\varphi}{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})=0

Moreover, for some putative extension to
m
th order in
\hbar
,
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}
, we have

\varphi_{/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\qquad\text{if and only if}\qquad d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})\ .

Thus, there exists an extension

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

First we show that the obstruction,
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
, is closed. The Bianchi identity for the DG Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{\varphi}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}
implies that

\big[\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}},d_{\varphi}\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}},\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}]\big]=0\penalty\ .

Taking the coefficient of
\hbar^{m}
in the above tells us that
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
must be closed.

Now, consider our putative lift

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\hbar^{k}\varphi_{k}\ \in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{1,(1)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}\penalty\ ,

whose restriction to
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
,

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}:=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\mod\hbar^{m}=\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\hbar^{k}\varphi_{k}\ \in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{1,(1)}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}\penalty\ ,

satisfies the Maurer–Cartan equations. Then
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}
satisfies the Maurer–Cartan equations if and only if they hold at order
\hbar^{m}
, which is equivalent to the condition that

(6.4)

d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}+[\varphi,\varphi_{m}]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-k}]=0\ .

Thus, by the definition of the obstruction cocyle, the data
\varphi_{m}
defining such an extension is equivalent to an element
\varphi_{m}\in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{1,(1)}
satisfying
d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
, or equivalently a trivialization of the cohomology class
[{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]\in H^{2}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
.

Given two such extensions of
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
to order
\hbar^{m}
, denoted

\varphi_{/\hbar^{m+1}}\ ,\ \tilde{\varphi}_{/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

their corresponding elements
\varphi_{m},\tilde{\varphi}_{m}\in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{1,(1)}
evidently satisfy

d_{\varphi}(\varphi_{m}-\tilde{\varphi}_{m})={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})-{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})=0\penalty\ ,

so that they define a cocycle
\varphi_{m}-\tilde{\varphi}_{m}\in Z^{1}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
, and conversely the same argument implies any such cocycle
\zeta\in Z^{1}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}(\varphi)
added to a given
\varphi_{m}
yields an alternative extension
\tilde{\varphi}_{m}=\varphi_{m}+\zeta
which still satisfies
d_{\varphi}(\tilde{\varphi}_{m})={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
.

Furthermore, two such deformations are identified by an automorphism

e^{\hbar^{m}a}=\mathbbm{1}+\hbar^{m}a\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}},{\mathcal{Q}},\hbar^{m+1}}\penalty\ ,

with
a\in({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}^{\varphi})^{0,(0)}
if and only if
e^{\hbar^{m}a}\circ\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\circ e^{-\hbar^{m}a}=\widetilde{\varphi}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}
, which in turn implies that

\varphi_{m}-\tilde{\varphi}_{m}=d_{\varphi}a\ ,

noting the only non-trivial constraint is at order
\hbar^{m}
.

∎

6.2.
Deformation theory for families of algebras

In this section, as in the previous, we study the order-by-order deformation-obstruction theory of maps between families of operads but unlike the preceding section we allow the operads to be non-constant.

Let
{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
be operads internal to
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]{\textup{-Mod}}
, which we view as families of operads over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
. The space of
\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}
-algebra structures internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}
is controlled by the weight graded, DG Lie algebra internal to
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]{\textup{-Mod}}
, defined by

{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}=\bigoplus_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}^{(\ell)}\qquad\text{where}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}^{(\ell)}=\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}^{{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}}_{\hbar}}^{(\ell)},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar})=\bigoplus_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Hom_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar][{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]{\textup{-Mod}}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}^{{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}}_{\hbar}}^{(\ell)}(n),\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}(n))\ .

In terms of this grading, we have the following application of Proposition
5.9
:

Corollary 6.10
.

A
{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}
-algebra internal to
{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}
is equivalent to a solution to the Maurer-Cartan equations in
{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}
of pure weight
1
, that is,

{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}}({\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}})=\Hom_{\mathrm{Op}_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}({\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}})\subset{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}^{1,(1)}\penalty\ .

Given
\varphi_{\hbar}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}}({\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}})
, a
{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}
-algebra internal to
{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}
, we obtain a new DG Lie algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]

{\mathfrak{g}^{\varphi_{\hbar}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}=\left(\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar})\ ,\ [\cdot,\cdot]_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}\ ,\ d_{\varphi_{\hbar}}\right)

where
d_{\varphi_{\hbar}}=d_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}+[\varphi_{\hbar},\cdot]_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}:{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\to{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}[1]
as in Definition
5.12
. In particular, we obtain the operadic deformation-obstruction complex

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar})=({\mathfrak{g}^{\varphi_{\hbar}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\ ,\ d_{\varphi_{\hbar}})\ .

More generally, for any fixed order
m\geq 0
there is a natural DG Lie algebra over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}

{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}/\hbar^{m+1}}={\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}}\qquad\text{and similarly}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}/\hbar^{m+1}}^{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}={\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}}^{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}

a DG Lie algebra defined for each
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}}({\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}})

which manifestly controls the deformation obstruction theory of
\mathcal{P}_{/\hbar^{m+1}}
-algebras internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{/\hbar^{m+1}}
, where we define

{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}={\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}={\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}\ .

Taking
m=0
in this construction recovers the specializations of the DG Lie algebras
{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}
and
{\mathfrak{g}^{\varphi_{\hbar}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}
at
\hbar=0
, which agree with the usual DG Lie algebras associated to the pair of operads
(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0})
and the algebra
\varphi=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
, that is, we have

{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{g}^{\varphi_{\hbar}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}^{\varphi}\ .

We now introduce the analogue of Definition
6.2
of the space of deformations, and its generalization to the stack of deformations in Equation
6.1
, to the present setting. Let
(S,\mathfrak{m}_{S})
be a local Artinian ring with a closed embedding
{\textup{Spec\ }}S{\ \hookrightarrow\ }\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
sending the closed point of
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
to the origin of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

The following definition describes the space of deformations of
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{0})
over
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
to a
\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}
:

Definition 6.11
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be an
\mathcal{P}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}
. The space of deformations of
\varphi
over
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
, as an
\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}
, is defined as the fibre product

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(S)={{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\ .

Similarly, the stack of such deformations is defined as the fibre product of Maurer-Cartan stacks

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(S)={{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\underset{{{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi_{0}\}\ ,

where the Maurer-Cartan stack is defined as the quotient stack

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\ \big/\ \mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}\right]

and the group
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}=\exp({\mathfrak{g}}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}\mathfrak{m}_{S})
, the formal exponential of the nilpotent Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}\mathfrak{m}_{S}
—analogous to the definition of the Maurer-Cartan stack in Equation
6.2
.

In particular, the scheme and stack of deformations over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}
are given by

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})={{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

and

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})={{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}})\underset{{{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

respectively. Note that

{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})\xrightarrow{\cong}{{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})\ ,

since the maximal ideal of the local ring is trivial, and thus the group
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}
is as well. Thus, the fibre product condition in the definition of
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(R)
requires strict agreement of the restriction to the central fibre with the initial algebra
\varphi
, rather than up to some isomorphism.

Compared to the simpler descriptions of Propositions
6.3
and
6.6
, the Maurer–Cartan stacks does not make reference to the nilpotent DG Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0},{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}}^{\varphi}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{S}
. Nevertheless, we will be able to establish the analogue of Proposition
6.9
on the order by order deformation theory.

6.2.1.
Order by order deformation theory

Before we proceed, a warning for the reader:

Warning 6.12
.

For the remainder of this section, we will assume that
\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}
are such that at each arity
n
, the
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
-modules
\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(n)
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}(n)
are trivializable, and in the DG case we assume that the analogue is true for the underlying
{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}
module in each cohomological degree.

We will refer to this data formally in terms of the following definition:

Definition 6.13
.

By a trivialization of the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-module of an operad
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
or a cooperad
{\mathcal{C}}_{\hbar}
over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
we mean a choice of trivialization at each arity
n
of the
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
-modules
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}(n)
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\hbar}(n)
,
i.e.
am isomorphism of
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
-modules

{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(n)\cong{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(n)\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathcal{C}}_{\hbar}(n)\cong{\mathcal{C}}_{0}(n)\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ .

Note that this is much weaker than a trivialization of the operadic or cooperadic structure.

We fix such trivializations of the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules of
\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}_{\hbar}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}
, thus giving an identification

(6.5)

{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\cong{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\qquad\text{with}\qquad d_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\hbar^{j}d_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\hbar^{j}[\cdot,\cdot]_{j}\ ,

for maps
d_{j}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}[-1]
and
[\cdot,\cdot]:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}^{\otimes 2}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
. The zeroth order maps
d_{0}:g_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}[-1]
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{0}:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}^{\otimes 2}\to{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
are the usual DG Lie algebra structure on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
, while the higher order in
\hbar
terms describe the deformation of this DG Lie algebra as the operads
\mathcal{P}_{0}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
deform over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
.

Recall from the preceding section that when
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
are constant, there is no obstruction to deforming
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{0})
over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{2}
. This is because one always has a trivial deformation of
\varphi
by extending it as a constant map of operads over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{2}
. It is only after choosing a non-trivial deformation of
\varphi
to first order that one might encounter a potential obstruction.

However, in the current setting,
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
are non-constant and there is no canonical extension of
\varphi
as a constant. Thus, one should expect even the first order deformation of
\varphi
to be potentially obstructed.

We proceed to repeat the analysis preceding Proposition
6.9
in the present setting: For
m\geq 1
, we define the generalized obstruction cochain associated with
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
by

(6.6)

{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}):=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-k}]-\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}\varphi_{m-j}-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k=0}^{m-j}\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-j-k}]_{j}\ \in{\mathfrak{g}}^{2,(2)}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}}\ .

We now state the common generalization of Propositions
6.8
and
6.9
in this setting:

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be a
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
. Then
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
satisfies
d_{\varphi}{\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})=0
, and for
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}
we have

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\qquad\text{if and only if}\qquad d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})\ .

Thus, there exists an extension

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

We repeat the argument of Proposition
6.9
, keeping track of the additional
\hbar
-dependence coming from the families
{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}
, and the fixed trivializations of their underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules in the sense of Definition
6.13
. In particular, via these trivializations one has an identification of
{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}
-modules

{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\;\cong\;{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}

under which the differential and bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}
admit expansions

d_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}d_{j}\qquad\text{and}\qquad[\cdot,\cdot]_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}[\cdot,\cdot]_{j}

for
d_{j}
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{j}
defined on the underlying vector space
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
, as in the description following Equation
6.5
.

Fix
m\geq 1
and suppose that

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}=\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}\hbar^{k}\varphi_{k}\;\in\;({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}})^{1,(1)}\otimes_{{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m})

is a Maurer-Cartan element, that is

d_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})+\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}},\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}]_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}=0\ \in({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}})^{2,(2)}\otimes_{{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m}).

Consider an arbitrary lift

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\hbar^{k}\varphi_{k}\ \in({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}})^{1,(1)}\otimes_{{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m+1}),

and expand the Maurer-Cartan equation modulo
\hbar^{m+1}
. The Maurer-Cartan equations for
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}
modulo
\hbar^{m}
are precisely the same as those for
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
, which hold by hypothesis. So it remains to check the vanishing of the coefficient of
\hbar^{m}
. Using the expansions of
d_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}
fixed above and collecting the terms of order
\hbar^{m}
gives

d_{0}\varphi_{m}+[\varphi,\varphi_{m}]_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-k}]_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_{j}\varphi_{m-j}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k=0}^{m-j}\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{k},\varphi_{m-j-k}]_{j}=0.

By the Definition of
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
in Equation
6.6
, this is equivalent to

(6.7)

d_{\varphi}(\varphi_{m})={\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}),

where
d_{\varphi}:=d_{0}+[\varphi,-]_{0}
is the differential on the operadic deformation complex

C^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
. Thus, a lift
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}
is Maurer-Cartan
modulo
\hbar^{m+1}
if and only if
d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}={\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
, as claimed. In particular, an extension exists if and only if the cochain

{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
is exact, equivalently

[{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\in H^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi).

As in Proposition
6.9
, we use the Bianchi identity to show that
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
is in general a
d_{\varphi}
-cocycle. Since
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
satisfies the Maurer-Cartan equation up to order
\hbar^{m}
, we have the expansion

(6.8)

d_{\hbar}\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\;+\;\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}},\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}]\;=\;\hbar^{\,m}\,{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})\;+\;\textup{terms of order }\hbar^{\,m+1}\,.

The Bianchi identity modulo
\hbar^{m+1}
is given by

d_{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}\!\Big(d_{\hbar}\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\;+\;\frac{1}{2}[\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}},\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}]\Big)\;=\;0\,.

so that applying the differential
d_{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}}
to both sides of Equation
6.8
, the vanishing at order
\hbar^{m}
is equivalent to

d_{\varphi}\!\Big({\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})\Big)\;=\;0\,,

and thus
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
is indeed closed with respect to the differential
d_{\varphi}
.

Finally, suppose
[{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0
so that extensions exist. If
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}
and
\tilde{\varphi}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\tilde{\varphi}_{m}
are two such extensions, then we have

d_{\varphi}(\tilde{\varphi}_{m}-\varphi_{m})=0,

so
\delta:=\tilde{\varphi}_{m}-\varphi_{m}
is a
1
-cocycle. Moreover,
\delta
is a coboundary,
\delta=d_{\varphi}(\eta)
, if and only if the two lifts are related by the action of

\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}}
at order
\hbar^{m}
, where the group
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{R}}
is as in Definition
6.11
. Thus, isomorphism classes of such extensions again form a torsor under
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
, as claimed.
∎

6.3.
Deformation theory for graded algebras

Recall the distinguished multiplicative group
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
that acts on
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
by scaling
\hbar
with unit weight. Let
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
be graded operads over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
, which we think of as families of operads over the stack
[\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}/\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}]
.

We write
{\mathcal{P}}_{0}\coloneqq{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
for the central fibre at
0\in[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}]
and
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\coloneqq{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{1}
for the fibre over the closed point
1\in[\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}]
.

The fibre operad
{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
is naturally equipped with an (ascending) filtration
F_{\bullet}{\mathcal{P}}_{1}
, induced by the grading on
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
, while the fibre operad
{\mathcal{P}}_{0}
is a graded operad over
{\mathbb{K}}
. These two fibres are related by

\textup{gr}^{\bullet}_{F}{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\cong{\mathcal{P}}_{0}\penalty\ .

We have, more or less, just recalled the Rees construction for operads. Namely, any filtered
{\mathbb{K}}
-linear operad,
{\mathcal{P}}
defines a graded
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
-operad
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
via

{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}={\textup{Rees}}_{\hbar}\,{\mathcal{P}}_{1}=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{m}{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\hbar^{m}\penalty\ ,

such that

{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\cong{\mathcal{P}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathcal{P}}_{0}\cong\textup{gr}^{\bullet}_{F}{\mathcal{P}}\penalty\ .

In paticular, if
{\mathcal{P}}
is trivially filtered,
F_{m}{\mathcal{P}}={\mathcal{P}}
for any
m\geq 0
and zero otherwise, then
\textup{gr}_{F}{\mathcal{P}}\cong{\mathcal{P}}
and the associated Rees operad is the constant extension

{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}={\mathcal{P}}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ .

Thus, taking
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
to be the Rees operads of trivially filtered operads
{\mathcal{P}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}
, respectively, reduces to the results of section
6.1
.

Once again, let
(S,\mathfrak{m}_{S})
be a graded local Artinian ring with maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{S}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-equivariant, closed embedding
{\textup{Spec\ }}S{\ \hookrightarrow\ }\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
sending the closed point of
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
to the origin.

Definition 6.15
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be a graded
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
. The space of deformations of
\varphi
over
{\textup{Spec\ }}S
is defined the fibre product

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(S)={{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0},{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ .

Similarly the stack of such deformations is defined as the fibre product of Maurer–Cartan stacks

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(S)={{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar},\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0},{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

where the Maurer-Cartan stack is defined as the quotient stack

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}^{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}S)\ \big/\ \mathfrak{G}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}\right]

and the group
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{S}}=\exp({\mathfrak{g}}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}\mathfrak{m}_{S})
, the formal exponential of the nilpotent Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}\mathfrak{m}_{S}
—analogous to the definition of the Maurer-Cartan stack in Equation
6.2
.

In particular, the variety and stack of deformations over
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1}
are given by

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})={{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}/\hbar^{m+1}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

and

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})={{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}/\hbar^{m+1}})\underset{{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

respectively.

6.3.1.
Order by order deformation theory

Once again we return to the setting of Warning
6.12
and assume that the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}
admit trivializations. Moreover, we assume that these trivializations are
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-equivariant.

We also have the graded analogue of the preceding Theorem
6.14
.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be a graded
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
. Then there exists an extension

\varphi_{/\hbar^{m+1}}\ \in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
, the component of
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
that has degree
-m
.

Proof.

This is the graded analogue of Theorem
6.14
. We again work under fixed trivializations of the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules, in the sense of Definition
6.13
, so that we may expand the differential and bracket on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}
as
d_{\hbar}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}d_{j}
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\hbar}=\sum_{j\geq 0}\hbar^{j}[\cdot,\cdot]_{j}
, where the components
d_{j}
and
[\cdot,\cdot]_{j}
have degree
-j
.

Let
m\geq 1
and let
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m}))
be a graded
m
th order deformation in the sense of Definition
6.15
. By construction,

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
lies in the degree
0
subspace
({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m}))^{1,(1),\langle 0\rangle}
,
and any prospective lift

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}\ \in({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}}{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m+1}))^{1,(1),\langle 0\rangle}

must again lie in degree
0
. Equivalently,
\varphi_{m}
must have degree
-m
, since the factor
\hbar^{m}
contributes degree
m
in our Rees conventions.

Expanding the Maurer-Cartan equation exactly as in the proof of Theorem
6.14
shows that

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}
is Maurer-Cartan modulo
\hbar^{m+1}
if and only if the coefficient
of
\hbar^{m}
vanishes, or equivalently

d_{\varphi}(\varphi_{m})={\textup{Obs}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}),

with both sides lying in the homogeneous degree
-m
subspace of
C^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
. Thus, an extension exists if and only if

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\in H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi).

When the obstruction class vanishes, the same subtraction argument as in Theorem
6.14
shows that two choices of
\varphi_{m}
differ by a
1
-cocycle of degree
-m
, and that changing
\varphi_{m}
by a degree
-m
coboundary corresponds precisely to the adjoint action of
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},(\hbar)}
, as in Definition
6.15
. Hence, the isomorphism classes of graded extensions form a torsor for

H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
.
∎

6.4.
Formal deformations of algebras

The deformation theory of the preceeding sections also makes sense for the complete local ring
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
. Write
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}}={\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
for the restriction of the family
{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar}
to the formal neighborhood of the origin of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
, and analogously for
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}
.

Proposition 6.17
.

We have an isomorphism of DG Lie algebras over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}

\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hbar},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hbar}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\penalty\ .

Proof.

By construction.
∎

Definition 6.18
.

The schemes
{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
and
{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,\textup{gr}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
of deformations and graded deformations, respectively, over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
are defined as

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})={{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathcal{Q}_{\hat{\hbar}}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,\quad\text{ and }\quad{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,\textup{gr}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})={{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0},{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ ,

generalizing Definition
6.11
. Similarly the stacks of deformations,
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, and graded deformations,
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,\textup{gr}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
are defined as

{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})={{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathcal{Q}_{\hat{\hbar}}})\underset{{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\ ,\quad\text{ and }\quad{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,\textup{gr}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})={{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}})\underset{{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{0},{\mathcal{Q}}_{0}})}{\times}\{\varphi\}\penalty\ .

The Maurer–Cartan stacks are defined as the quotients

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathcal{Q}_{\hat{\hbar}}})=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathcal{Q}_{\hat{\hbar}}})/\mathfrak{G}_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}}^{0,(0)}\right]

and

{{\mathfrak{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}})=\left[{{\textup{MC}}}_{\textup{gr}}({\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}}})\ \big/\ \mathfrak{G}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}}\right]\penalty\ ,

for
\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}=(\hbar)\subset{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
and the groups
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}}
and
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0),\langle 0\rangle}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{\hat{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{\hat{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}}
from Definitions
6.11
and
6.15
.

The results of Theorems
6.14
and
6.16
most naturally provide information about spaces of deformations over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
, in the following sense:

Proposition 6.19
.

There are natural isomorphisms of varieties and stacks

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim_{m}{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim_{m}{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})\ ,

as well as their graded variants

{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim_{m}{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\xrightarrow{\cong}\lim_{m}{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})\ .

Proof.

Given a formal deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, for each
m\geq 1
we can consider its truncation
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}:=\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
. Each
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
lies in
{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
, and these truncations are clearly compatible as
m
varies. This defines a natural map

{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\to\lim_{m}{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}),

sending
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}
to the projective system
\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}_{m\geq 1}
. This map is injective (if two formal deformations agree to all finite orders, they are equal as formal power series) and it remains to show it is surjective.

For surjectivity, let
\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}_{m\geq 1}
be a compatible family with
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\equiv\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\pmod{\hbar^{m}}
for each
m
. By construction, in terms of the fixed trivializations of the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules of
{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}
, we can write

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\;=\;\varphi+\hbar\,\varphi_{1}+\hbar^{2}\,\varphi_{2}+\cdots+\hbar^{m-1}\,\varphi_{m-1}\ ,

for some elements
\varphi_{i}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
(in particular
\varphi_{0}=\varphi
is the original
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra structure) and similarly

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\;=\;\varphi+\hbar\,\varphi_{1}+\hbar^{2}\,\varphi_{2}+\cdots+\hbar^{m-1}\,\varphi_{m-1}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}\ ,

for some
\varphi_{m}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
satisfying

d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}\;=\;{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\!\big(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\big)\ .

In particular, we obtain an infinite sequence
\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\geq 0}
with
\varphi_{0}=\varphi
and
d_{\varphi}\varphi_{m}={\textup{Obs}}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
for each
m\geq 1
. The resulting formal power series

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\;:=\;\varphi+\hbar\,\varphi_{1}+\hbar^{2}\,\varphi_{2}+\hbar^{3}\,\varphi_{3}+\cdots

defines an element of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
. By construction, this
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}
is a solution to the Maurer-Cartan equation
to arbitrary order
in
\hbar
, hence in fact

d_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}(\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})+\frac{1}{2}\big[\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\big]_{{\mathfrak{g}}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}}=0

as a formal power series. In other words,
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is a formal deformation whose truncation at each order
m
is precisely
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
. Thus, the map
\lim_{m}{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\to{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
is also surjective, and thus an isomorphism.

Finally, the isomorphism of deformation stacks follows by passing to the quotient by gauge equivalences. Indeed, the pro-nilpotence of the automorphism DG Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}^{0,(0)}
ensures that the construction of the gauge group
\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{R}}=\exp({\mathfrak{g}}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}\otimes\mathfrak{m}_{R})
commutes with filtered inverse limits, so that we have

\lim_{m}\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{(m)}}\;\cong\;\mathfrak{G}^{0,(0)}_{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}},\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}}

for the maximal ideals
\mathfrak{m}_{(m)}
of
{\mathbb{K}[\![\hbar]\!]}/\hbar^{m}
and
\mathfrak{m}_{\hat{\hbar}}=(\hbar)
of
{\mathbb{K}[\![\hbar]\!]}
. Thus, the above bijection on Maurer–Cartan solutions descends to an isomorphism of the relevant quotient stacks. This yields the stated isomorphism
\lim_{m}{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})\cong{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
.

The graded case follows from the same argument,
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

In particular, we obtain the following key corollaries about spaces of formal (graded) deformations:

Corollary 6.20
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
a
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
such that
H^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=\{0\}
. Then there exists a formal deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
.

Proof.

For each
m\geq 1
, consider the inductive extension problem

{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m+1}))\times_{{\textup{Def}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}_{\varphi}({{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}/(\hbar^{m}))}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ .

By Theorem
6.14
, the obstruction to extending from order
m-1
to order
m
is the class of
{\textup{Obs}}_{m}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})
in
H^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
.
Since
H^{2}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=0
, every such obstruction class vanishes, so extensions exist at every order. Starting from
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar}=\varphi
, we obtain a compatible system of deformations
\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}_{m\geq 1}
. Passing to the limit and using Proposition
6.19
, we obtain an element

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\ ,

as claimed.
∎

Corollary 6.21
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be a
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
such that
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=\{0\}
. Then if there exists a formal deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, it is unique up to isomorphism.

Proof.

Fix
m\geq 1
and an
m^{th}
-order deformation
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}
. Given
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi_{m}
and
\varphi^{\prime}_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}=\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}+\hbar^{m}\varphi^{\prime}_{m}
are two
(m+1)^{th}
-order extensions, by the torsor statement in Theorem
6.14
their isomorphism classes differ by an element of
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
. Under the assumption
H^{1}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=0
, this torsor is trivial, so there is at most one isomorphism class of extension at each order. By induction on
m
, any two compatible inverse systems of truncations
\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}
are isomorphic at every finite stage, hence define the same point of the limit stack. By Proposition
6.19
, the resulting formal deformation over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
is therefore unique up to isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 6.22
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
a graded
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
such that
H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=\{0\}
for all
m
.

Then, there exists a formal, graded deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
.

Proof.

This is proved as in Corollary
6.20
, using the graded extension criterion of Theorem
6.16
. Namely, at step
m
the obstruction lies in
H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
, which vanishes by hypothesis, hence graded extensions exist for all

m
. Taking the inverse limit and applying the graded limit statement of Proposition
6.19
gives a formal graded deformation over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
.
∎

Corollary 6.23
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
be a graded
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
such that
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=\{0\}
for any
m\geq 1
.

Then if there exists a formal, graded deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, it is unique up to isomorphism.

Proof.

Fix
m\geq 1
. By the torsor statement in Theorem
6.16
, isomorphism classes of graded extensions from order
m
to
m+1
form a torsor for
H^{1,\langle m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
, which is trivial by hypothesis. Thus graded extensions are unique up to isomorphism at each stage. Passing to
the inverse limit and using Proposition
6.19
(graded form) yields uniqueness of the resulting formal graded deformation over
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
.
∎

Corollary 6.24
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0}}^{\textup{gr}}(\mathcal{Q}_{0})
a graded
\mathcal{P}_{0}
-algebra internal to
\mathcal{Q}_{0}
such that
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)=\{0\}
for all
m\neq m_{0}
, with
m_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Then if there exists a formal, graded deformation
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, the stack of formal, graded deformations
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m_{0}\rangle}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

Assume a formal graded deformation exists. By Theorem
6.16
, at the
m
th extension step the set of isomorphism classes of graded extensions is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}(\varphi)
. By hypothesis, this torsor is trivial for
m\neq m_{0}
, and potentially nontrivial only at
m=m_{0}
. Consequently the only degree of freedom in the inverse system of truncations occurs in degree
-m_{0}
, and it is governed precisely by the action of
H^{1,\langle-m_{0}\rangle}(\varphi)
.

Passing to the limit via Proposition
6.19
, we conclude that
{\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\mathcal{P}_{\hbar}},{\mathcal{Q}_{\hbar}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m_{0}\rangle}(\varphi)
.
∎

7.
Quantizations via deformation theory

We will now apply the results of the previous section to the problem of deformation quantization. First, we revisit the traditional notion of deformation quantization of Poisson algebras, and outline the application of operadic deformation theory in this setting to recover some well-known results about their spaces of quantizations. In the following sections, we apply these results to the deformation theory of coisson algebras to give analogous descriptions of their spaces of quantizations.

7.1.
Quantization of Poisson algebras

7.1.1.

There is a natural increasing filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\text{cl}}{\textup{Ass}}
on the associative operad, defined by

F_{-2}^{\text{cl}}{\textup{Ass}}(2)=\{0\}\quad\subset\quad F_{-1}^{\text{cl}}{\textup{Ass}}(2)={\textup{Lie}}(2)\quad\subset\quad F_{0}^{\text{cl}}{\textup{Ass}}(2)={\textup{Ass}}(2)\penalty\ ,

i.e.
, the standard inclusion of the commutator into the space of bilinear maps on 2-ary operations. Using the fact that the filtration on 2-ary operations is naturally split by taking anti-symmetric and symmetric components (given by commutator and anti-commutator) and that these generate
{\textup{Ass}}(n)
under composition, we define the filtration
F^{\text{cl}}_{\bullet}{\textup{Ass}}(n)
by

F^{\text{cl}}_{-k}{\textup{Ass}}(n)=\{\mu\in{\textup{Ass}}(n)\ |\ \text{every binary tree presentation of $\mu$ has at least $k$ commutators}\}\ .

The following is a standard result, following essentially from the presentation of the Rees operad below.

Proposition 7.1
.

The associated graded operad
\textup{gr}_{\bullet}^{F^{\text{cl}}}({\textup{Ass}})
is isomorphic to the Poisson operad,

{\textup{Pois}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\textup{gr}_{\bullet}^{F^{\text{cl}}}({\textup{Ass}})\ \in\mathrm{Op}(\Vect_{\mathbb{K}}^{\textup{gr}})\penalty\ ,

as graded operads, where the Poisson operad is equipped with the auxiliary grading from
2.3.4
.

We define

(7.1)

{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}={\textup{Rees}}_{\hbar}^{F^{\text{cl}}_{\bullet}}({\textup{Ass}})=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{m}^{\text{cl}}{\textup{Ass}}\cdot\hbar^{m}\penalty\ ,

The Rees operad,
{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}
, is known as the Beilinson–Drinfeld operad, see
[
MS18
]
and
[
CG16
]
.

We write

{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}}\coloneqq{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\penalty\ ,

for its completion, which is a
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-operad.

Let
A_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}{\textup{-Mod}}
, then we write
{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})
for the endomorphism operad of
A_{\hat{\hbar}}
internal to the symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}{\textup{-Mod}}

Remark 7.2
.

The data of
\varphi_{\hbar}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}}}({\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})
(or its graded counterpart) corresponds to a (graded) associative multiplication

m_{\hbar}:A_{\hat{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}A_{\hat{\hbar}}\rightarrow A_{\hat{\hbar}}\penalty\ ,

and a (graded) Lie bracket

[\cdot,\cdot]_{\hbar}:A_{\hat{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}A_{\hat{\hbar}}\rightarrow A_{\hat{\hbar}}\penalty\ ,

that is a biderivation of
m
and satisfies
\hbar[a,b]_{\hbar}=m_{\hbar}(a,b)-m_{\hbar}(b,a)
for any
a,b\in A
. In the limit
\hbar=0
,
m_{0}:A_{0}\otimes A_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}
is commutative and so
(m_{0},[\cdot,\cdot]_{0})
defines a (graded) Poisson algebra structure on the central fibre
A_{0}
.

7.1.2.

In light of Remark
7.2
we make the following definition.

Definition 7.3
.

Given a Poisson algebra
(A,\varphi)
, a
quantization
,
(A_{\hat{\hbar}}\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
, is a

(i)

flat
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module
A_{\hat{\hbar}}
, complete in the
\hbar
-adic topology, with an isomorphism of the central fibre
A_{0}=A\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}A
