(ii)

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}}}({\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{\hat{\hbar}}))

such that the central fibre Poisson algebra
(A_{0},\varphi_{0})
is identified with the Poisson algebra
(A,\varphi)
.

If
(A,\varphi)
is a graded Poisson algebra, we say that a quantization
(A_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
is a
graded quantization
if
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}}}({\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})
and the isomorphism
(A_{0},\varphi_{0})\cong(A,\varphi)
is an isomorphism of graded Poisson algebras.

Now, take
A_{\hat{\hbar}}
to be the trivial family
A\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
. Given a
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Pois}}}({\textup{End}}(A))
, consider a deformation

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}},{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})}(\varphi)\penalty\ .

By construction,
A_{\hbar}
is a flat,
\hbar
-adic
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module with central fibre equal to
A
and
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}}}({\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}}))
is such that
\varphi_{0}=\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Pois}}}({\textup{End}}(A))
. Thus,
(A_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
is a quantization of
(A,\varphi)
. Indeed, the scheme of deformations parameterises all quantizations of
(A,\varphi)
whose underlying
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module is the trivial family.

Let
S
be the Artinian local ring,
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
, or the complete local ring
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
.
Therefore, we make the following definitions:

(7.2)

{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)={\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}},{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})}(S)\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}(S)={\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}},{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})}(S)\penalty\ ,

for the schemes of quantizations and graded quantizations, with the stipulation that the underlying
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module is
A\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
.

We define the stacks of quantizations and graded quantizations as

(7.3)

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)={\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}},{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})}(S)\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}(S)={\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hat{\hbar}}},{\textup{End}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(A_{\hat{\hbar}})}(S).

Remark 7.4
.

The stacks
{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)
(and its graded counterpart) actually parameterise the isomorphism classes of all (graded) quantizations of
(A,\varphi)
. This is because the underlying
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module of every quantization is isomorphic to
A\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
and so every quantization of
(A,\varphi)
has a representative whose underlying
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-module is the trivial family.

One may think of our scheme of quantizations as a kind of partial gauge fixing of an
a priori
larger, scheme of quantizations, which in addition parameterizes the data of the central fibre isomorphism.

Remark 7.5
.

Unlike the other source operads we have considered, the
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-operad
{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}
is not quadratic and, therefore, not Koszul. To apply the results of Section
6
, we need to resolve
{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}
by a DG, Koszul operad
{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}}^{{\textup{DG}}}
. Since quantization of Poisson algebras is not the focus of our work we will not address this complication presently, but point the interested reader to
[
MS18
,
CG16
]
for more details.

7.1.3.

Applying our order by order deformation theory results, we have:

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Pois}}}({\textup{End}}(A))
a Poisson algebra and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
a quantization defined modulo
\hbar^{m}
. Then there exists an extension

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\ \in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\textup{BD}_{\hbar}},{\textup{End}}(A_{\hbar})}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2}_{{\textup{Pois}}}(A,\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1}_{{\textup{Pois}}}(A,\varphi)
.

Corollary 7.7
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Pois}}}({\textup{End}}(A))
a Poisson algebra such that
H^{2}_{{\textup{Pois}}}(A,\varphi)=\{0\}
. Then there exists a quantization
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.20
to the present setting.
∎

We will say that a Poisson algebra
A
is symplectic if the corresponding affine variety
{\textup{Spec\ }}A
is symplectic, and in particular smooth. We have the following important corollary:

Theorem 7.8
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Pois}}}({\textup{End}}(A))
be symplectic. Let
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
be a quantization modulo
\hbar^{m}
. If the obstruction class vanishes, then the space of extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{2}_{{\textup{dR}}}({\textup{Spec\ }}A)
, the second de Rham cohomology of
{\textup{Spec\ }}A
.

Proof.

The additional content of this Theorem is the identification of Poisson cohomology and de Rham cohomology, which can be understood structurally as follows: a Poisson structure on
A
determines a Lie algebroid structure on
\Omega^{1}_{A}
such that the anchor map
\alpha:\Omega^{1}_{A}\to\Theta_{A}
is given by contraction with the Poisson tensor. Moreover, the (reduced) Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on this Lie algebroid, with coefficients in the module
A
, recovers the (reduced) geometric Poisson cochains on
A
in the sense of Definition
5.27
, by Corollary
5.28
.

In the symplectic case, the (reduced) geometric Poisson cochains on
A
is quasi-isomorphic to the operadic Poisson cochains which feature in Theorem
7.6
, by Corollary
5.29
, since
A
is smooth. On the other hand, the symplectic hypothesis implies that the anchor map
\alpha
defines an isomorphism of Lie algebroids to
\Theta_{A}
, and the (reduced) Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on
\Theta_{A}
with coefficients in
A
is canonically identified with the (truncated) de Rham complex.

∎

This result is, of course, evocative of the classical results on quantization of symplectic manifolds, see
[
dWL83
,
Fed94
,
Kon03
]
as well as
[
BK04
]
for the algebraic setting. In our context, their main result implies that

(7.4)

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\cong\hbar H^{2}_{{\textup{dR}}}({\textup{Spec\ }}{A}){[\![\hbar]\!]}

with an explicit isomorphism given by the noncommutative period map of
[
BK04
]
.

Remark 7.9
.

From the perspective of this work, the stronger result of
[
BK04
]
, implies that there exist compatible trivializations of the family of torsors constructed at each order by Theorem
7.8
.

7.2.
The filtration on the chiral operad

7.2.1.

We now describe the analogue in the chiral setting of the classical filtration on the associative operad introduced in
7.1.1
, following section 3.2 of
[
BD04
]
. To do so, it will be convenient to return to the convention of labelling the arity of operations in the operad by
I\in{\textup{fSet}}
.

Given a
T\in{\textup{fSet}}
such that
I{\ \twoheadrightarrow\ }T
, one can define a diagonal embedding

\Delta^{(I/T)}:X^{T}\rightarrow X^{I}\penalty\ ,

where the embedding is specified by
I{\ \twoheadrightarrow\ }T
.

Recall from 3.1.6 of
loc. cit.
that a D-module
M\in{D}(X^{I})
is called
special
if it admits a finite, increasing filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}M
such that each of the subquotients
\textup{gr}^{\ell}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}M
is isomorphic to a finite direct sum of objects of the form
\Delta^{(I/T)}_{*}\omega_{X^{T}}
for
I{\ \twoheadrightarrow\ }T
a finite quotient of
I
of cardinality
-\ell
. In particular, the graded degree
\ell
component of the associated graded has support of dimension
-\ell
, for each
-|I|\leq\ell\leq 0
, with
F_{-|I|-1}^{\textup{sp}}M=0
and
F_{\ell}^{\textup{sp}}M=M
for
\ell>0
.

In particular, we have

Proposition 7.10
.

The object
j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\in{D}(X^{I})
is special.

Proof.

This is Lemma 3.1.7 in
[
BD04
]
.
∎

We let
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}
denote the associated special filtration; in particular, we have

F_{-1}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\qquad\text{and}\qquad F_{-|I|}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\ ,

so that the corresponding top and bottom degrees of the associated graded are given by

(7.5)

\textup{gr}^{-1}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=\Delta_{*}^{(I)}\omega_{X}\otimes{\textup{Lie}}(|I|)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\textup{gr}^{-|I|}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=M^{\boxtimes I}\ .

Note that this induces a canonical bounded, increasing filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)
on the chiral endomorphism operad
P^{\rm ch}(M)={\textup{End}}_{{D}(X)^{\rm ch}}(M)
, defined by

\displaystyle F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)

\displaystyle\equiv F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I},\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)

\displaystyle:=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/((F_{-k-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)

,

where we have used the natural identification

j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I}\cong(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}\ .

Note that we have shifted the degrees of the filtration relative to the definition of Equation 3.2.4.2 of
[
BD04
]
, so that the induced grading on the associated grading is the auxiliary grading introduced in Equation
3.3
, rather than that of
loc. cit.
; this ensures that the binary commutative product is in degree 0 while the binary Poisson bracket operation is in degree
-1
, in analogy with the finite type case.

In particular, in our conventions we have the following Proposition, which is stated in
[
BD04
]
in the alternative grading in the paragraph following Equation 3.2.4.2:

Proposition 7.11
.

Suppose
M\in{D}(X)
is projective. There are natural isomorphisms

\textup{gr}_{0}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\xrightarrow{\cong}P^{!}(M)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\textup{gr}_{|I|-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\xrightarrow{\cong}P^{*}(M)

such that the induced inclusion and projection

P^{!}(M)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}\cong\textup{gr}_{0}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\to P^{\rm ch}(M)\qquad\text{and}\qquad P^{\rm ch}(M)\to\textup{gr}_{|I|-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\cong P^{*}(M)

identify with the maps of colored operads
\alpha
and
\beta
of Equation
3.4
, respectively, restricted to the full, single-colored suboperad on the object
M
.

Proof.

We supply a proof to normalize the grading conventions: First, note that for
k=-1
we have
-k-2=-1
and
F_{-1}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}
so that
F_{-1}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=0
and thus

\textup{gr}_{0}^{F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=F_{0}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/((F_{-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)\ .

Now, note that again since
F_{-1}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}
we have

\displaystyle(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/((F_{-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})

\displaystyle=((F_{-1}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})/(F_{-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})

\displaystyle=(\textup{gr}_{-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})

\displaystyle\cong(\Delta_{*}^{(I)}\omega_{X})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}(I)

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{*}^{(I)}(M^{\otimes^{!}I})\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}(I)

where the first isomorphism is that of Equation
7.5
and the second follows by Kashiwara’s lemma. Thus, we obtain that
\textup{gr}_{0}^{F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=F_{0}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)
is given by

\displaystyle F^{0}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/((F_{-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(\Delta_{*}^{(I)}(M^{\otimes^{!}I}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}(I)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{D}(X)}(M^{\otimes^{!}I},M)\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}}{\textup{Lie}}_{\mathbb{K}}(I)

\displaystyle=(P^{!}(M)\otimes{\textup{Lie}})(I)

,

as desired, where the first isomorphism follows from the preceding identification of the source objects of the Hom space, and the latter follows again by Kashiwara’s lemma. It follows from Theorem 3.1.5 of
[
BD04
]
that the operadic composition in this suboperad of
P^{\rm ch}(M)
agrees with that of
P^{!}(M)\otimes{\textup{Lie}}
under the above identifications, and that this agrees with the definition of the map
\alpha
.

For
k=|I|-1
we have
-k-2=-|I|-1
and
F_{-|I|-1}^{\textup{sp}}=0
so that
F_{|I|-1}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=P^{\rm ch}(M)
, while for
k=|I|-2
we have
-k-2=-|I|
and
F^{\textup{sp}}_{-|I|}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}=\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}
so that

\displaystyle F_{|I|-2}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/((F_{-|I|}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/M^{\boxtimes I},\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M)

and thus the short exact sequence

M^{\boxtimes I}\to j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I}\to(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/M^{\boxtimes I}

induces, under application of the functor
\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(\cdot,\Delta_{*}^{(I)}M)
, the left exact sequence

\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I})\big/M^{\boxtimes I},\Delta_{*}^{(I)}M)\to\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}M^{\boxtimes I},\Delta_{*}^{(I)}M)\to\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(M^{\boxtimes I},\Delta_{*}^{(I)}M)

which is exact as long as
M
and thus
M^{\boxtimes I}
is a projective D-module, yielding the desired result:

\textup{gr}_{|I|-1}^{F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=F_{|I|-1}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)\big/F_{|I|-2}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)\cong P^{*}(M)(I)\ .

It is clear that this quotient identifies with the standard map of operads
\beta
, and in particular is compatible the operadic composition.
∎

In fact, the preceding Proposition follows as a special case of the following more general Theorem, stated in 3.2.5 of
[
BD04
]
under the same projectivity hypothesis. For our applications, we will need a strengthening of this result, and in particular of the preceding Proposition, to allow for the following more general class of D-modules:

Definition 7.12
.

A D-module
M\in{D}(X)
is called unital-projective if it admits a decomposition

M^{r}=\Omega^{d_{X}}_{X}\oplus P_{M}

where
P_{M}\in{D}^{r}(X)
is a countable direct sum of locally projective D-modules of finite rank.

Theorem 7.13
.

Let
X
be affine and let
{D}(X)_{\textup{up}}
denote the full subcategory of
{D}(X)
on unital-projective objects. There is an isomorphism of graded pseudo-tensor categories

\textup{gr}_{\bullet}^{F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}}{D}(X)_{\textup{up}}^{\rm ch}\xrightarrow{\cong}{D}(X)_{\textup{up}}^{c}\ ,

extending the identity functor on
{D}(X)_{\textup{up}}
.

Proof.

Let
I
be a finite set, let
\{L_{i}^{\textup{Tot}}\}_{i\in I},\ M^{\textup{Tot}}\in{D}(X)_{\textup{up}}
, and let
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I},\ M\in{D}(X)
be arbitrary choices of summand in their unital-projective presentation, so that each is either locally projective of finite rank, or simply equal to
\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
. Then each of
\{L_{i}\}
are in particular
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-flat and thus by the arguments of 3.2.5 in
[
BD04
]
, we have for each
k=0,...,|I|-1
an injection

\textup{gr}_{k}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}P^{\rm ch}(\{L_{i}\},M){\ \hookrightarrow\ }\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(\textup{gr}_{-k-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i}),\Delta_{*}M)=P^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle k\rangle}

and it remains to check surjectivity.

We recall from
loc. cit.
that we have

\textup{gr}_{-k-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\pi_{S},S)\in Q(I)\\
|S|=k+1\end{subarray}}\Delta^{(I/S)}_{*}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}(\otimes^{!}_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}))\otimes\bigotimes_{s\in S}{\textup{Lie}}(|I_{s}|)\ ,

which explains the equality with
P^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)^{\langle k\rangle}
above. Moreover, from the short exact sequence

\textup{gr}_{-k-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})\to(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})/F^{\textup{sp}}_{-k-2}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})\to(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})/F^{\textup{sp}}_{-k-1}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})\ ,

we apply the functor
\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(\cdot,\Delta_{*}M)
to obtain the long exact sequence

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\to F_{k-1}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(\{L_{i}\},M)\to F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(\{L_{i}\},M)\to\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(\textup{gr}_{-k-1}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i}),\Delta_{*}M)

\displaystyle\to\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X^{I})}((j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})/F^{\textup{sp}}_{-k-1}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i}),\Delta^{*}M)\to\cdots

.

Thus, it suffices to show the vanishing of
\Ext^{1}((j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})/F^{\textup{sp}}_{-k-1}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i}),\Delta_{*}M)
. Moreover, the source object
(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})/F^{\textup{sp}}_{-k-1}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})
is evidently filtered with associated graded components given by the associated graded components
\textup{gr}_{\ell}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i})
, so that by inductive application of the long exact sequence, it suffices to show

\Ext^{1}(\textup{gr}_{\ell}^{F^{\textup{sp}}_{\bullet}}(j_{*}j^{*}\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i}),\Delta_{*}M)=0\ .

Evidently the combinatorial factors of the Lie operations do not contribute to the
\Ext
, so that it remains to show the vanishing of

\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X^{I})}(\Delta^{(I/S)}_{*}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}(\otimes^{!}_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i})),\Delta^{I}_{*}M)\cong\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X^{S})}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}(\otimes^{!}_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}),\Delta^{S}_{*}M)\ ,

where the isomorphism follows by Kashiwara’s lemma.

Next, we note that whenever the fibre
I_{s}
contains at least one
i
for which
L_{i}
locally projective of finite rank, then the exterior factor
L_{s}:=\otimes^{!}_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}
is so as well, and otherwise
L_{s}=\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
. In this notation, it thus remains to show

\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X^{S})}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s},\Delta^{S}_{*}M)=0

where each
L_{s}
is either locally projective of finite rank, or equal to
\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
.

Note that each such
L_{s}
is in particular dualizable, as are the
\otimes^{!}
products of their duals, so that the functor
\Delta^{*}
is well defined on the source and we can apply the adjunction for the derived Hom

\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(X^{S})}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s},\Delta^{S}_{*}M)\cong\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(\Delta^{S*}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s}),M)\ .

We can compute the source object as

\Delta^{S*}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s})={{\mathbb{D}}}_{X^{S}}\Delta^{!}{{\mathbb{D}}}_{X^{S}}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s})={{\mathbb{D}}}_{X^{S}}(\otimes^{!,\mathbb{L}}_{s\in S}{{\mathbb{D}}}_{X}L_{s})\ .

We recall that the dual of a locally projective, finite rank D-module is again such, and similarly
\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
is self-dual, up to cohomological degree shifts which cancel with those relating
\otimes^{!,\mathbb{L}}_{s\in S}
to the plain tensor product
\otimes^{!}_{s\in S}
.

We now prove this
\Ext^{1}
vanishes by analyzing the various cases, corresponding to whether each of the
\{L_{i}\}_{i\in I}
or
M
is either locally projective of finite rank, or equal to
\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
. If at least one of the
L_{s}
is locally projective of finite rank, then, by the preceding paragraph, so is
{{\mathbb{D}}}_{X^{S}}(\otimes^{!,\mathbb{L}}_{s\in S}{{\mathbb{D}}}_{X}L_{s})
, and thus

\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X^{S})}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s},\Delta^{S}_{*}M)\cong\Ext^{1}_{{D}(X)}(\Delta^{S*}(\boxtimes_{s\in S}L_{s}),M)=0

since the source is locally projective and
X
is affine.

It remains to treat the case where we have
L_{s}=\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
for every
s\in S
. If
M
itself is equal to
\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
, then by the proof of Theorem 3.1.5 in
[
BD04
]
, the filtration is trivial and we obtain a canonical identification
P^{\rm ch}(\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}})\cong{\textup{Lie}}\cong P^{c}(\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}})
. Otherwise, if
M
is locally projective of finite rank, then
P^{c}(\{\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}\},M)=0
and our desired surjectivity is trivial, independent of the
\Ext^{1}
vanishing.

∎

Corollary 7.14
.

Let
M\in{D}(X)
be unital-projective. Then

\textup{gr}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\cong P^{c}(M)\penalty\ .

In particular, the preceding holds for
M={\mathcal{J}}A
with
A
a flat
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra.

7.2.2.

Let
(M,F^{M}_{\bullet}M)
be a filtered, unital-projective
D
-module. We assume there exists, and fix once and for all, a splitting of the filtration,
i.e.
, a grading compatible with the
D
-module structure,

(7.6)

M=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{\langle k\rangle}\ \in{D}(X)\qquad\text{such that}\qquad F_{k}M=\bigoplus_{l\leq k}M^{\langle l\rangle}\langle-l\rangle\ .

The corresponding Rees object is

M_{\hbar}={\textup{Rees}}_{\hbar}^{F^{M}_{\bullet}}(M)=\bigoplus_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}F^{M}_{m}M\cdot\hbar^{m}\in D(X)\boxtimes_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]{\textup{-Mod}}_{\textup{gr}},

and the splitting of (
7.6
) gives a trivialization,
i.e.
, a
\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}
-equivariant isomorphism

(7.7)

M_{\hbar}\xrightarrow{\sim}\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}M^{\langle k\rangle}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ .

7.2.3.

The filtration on
M
induces one on the operad
P^{\rm ch}(M)
, given by

(7.8)

F_{k}^{M}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=\sum_{p-q=k}\{\varphi\in P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)\ |\ \varphi(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}F_{p}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}M^{\boxtimes I})\subset\Delta^{(I)}_{*}F_{q}^{M}\ \}\penalty\ ,

where the induced filtration on
M^{\boxtimes I}
is defined by

(7.9)

F_{p}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}M^{\boxtimes I}=\sum_{p_{1}+\dots+p_{|I|}\leq p}F^{M}_{p_{1}}M\boxtimes\dots\boxtimes F_{p_{|I|}}^{M}M\penalty\ .

The splitting of the filtration on
M
induces a splitting of the filtration on
P^{{\rm ch}}_{M}
given by the bigrading

P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}\penalty\ ,

where the components are given by

(7.10)

P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}=\Hom_{{D}(X^{I})}(j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle},\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M^{\langle q\rangle})\ ,

with the filtration given by

F_{k}^{M}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{q-p\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}\ ,

and where we have used the induced splitting of the filtration
F_{\bullet}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}
on
M^{\boxtimes I}
given by

M^{\boxtimes I}=\bigoplus_{p\in\mathbb{Z}}(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle}\qquad\text{where}\qquad(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle}=\bigoplus_{p_{1}+...+p_{|I|}=p}M^{\langle p_{1}\rangle}\boxtimes\ldots\boxtimes M^{\langle p_{|I|}\rangle}\ .

Note that for each
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the projection to the
(p,q)
-graded component

(7.11)

\pi_{p,q}^{P^{\rm ch}(M)}:P^{\rm ch}(M)\to P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}\qquad\text{is given by}\qquad\mu\mapsto\pi_{q}^{M}\circ\mu\circ\iota_{p}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}\ ,

where
\iota_{p}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}:(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle}\to M^{\boxtimes I}
denotes the inclusion of the
p
th graded component of
M^{\boxtimes I}
and
\pi_{q}^{M}:M\to M^{\langle q\rangle}
denotes the projection to the
q
th graded component of
M
.

7.2.4.

There is a natural refinement of the special filtration of Section
7.2.1
to account for the underlying filtration on the object
M
, as fixed above. We define the filtration
F_{\bullet}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)
by

(7.12)

F_{k}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}(F_{\ell}^{M}\cap F^{\textup{sp}}_{k-\ell})P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)\ ,

where we have the following explicit description of the intersections occuring as summands

{\hskip-28.45274pt}(F_{\ell}^{M}\cap F_{j}^{{\textup{sp}}})P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=\sum_{p-q=\ell}\{\varphi\in P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)\ |\ \varphi(F_{p}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}M^{\boxtimes I})\subseteq\Delta_{*}F_{q}M\text{ , }\ \varphi((F_{-j-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}M^{\boxtimes I})=\{0\}\ \}

and the filtration
F_{\bullet}^{M^{\boxtimes I}}M^{\boxtimes I}
on
M^{\boxtimes I}
is as defined in Equation
7.9
.

7.2.5.

Proposition 7.15
.

The joint filtration
F_{\bullet}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)
on
P^{\rm ch}(M)
can be split, i.e., there exists a grading

P^{{\rm ch}}(M)=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{{\rm ch}}(M)^{\langle k\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}\qquad\text{such that}\qquad F_{k}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{i\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle i\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}\penalty\ .

The proof will consist primarily of bookkeeping; one chooses splittings of the special filtration restricted to each summand of the graded decomposition of Equation
7.10
, giving a splitting of the special filtration which is compatible with the graded decomposition in such a way that it can be naturally modified to define a splitting of the joint filtration.

We define the induced special filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
on the
(p,q)
-graded component
P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
as the intersection

F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}=P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}\cap F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\ .

Then the essential content of the proof of Proposition
7.15
is isolated in the following elementary lemma:

Lemma 7.16
.

The restriction of the projection
\pi_{p,q}^{P^{\rm ch}(M)}
of Equation
7.11
to
F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)
factors as

Proof.

Note that the intersection defining
F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
admits the following concrete description

{\hskip-42.67912pt}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}(I)\cap F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)(I)=\Hom_{{D}(X^{n})}\bigg((j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I}\otimes^{!}(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle})\big/((F_{-k-2}^{\textup{sp}}j^{(I)}_{*}j^{(I)*}\omega_{X}^{\boxtimes I})\otimes^{!}(M^{\boxtimes I})^{\langle p\rangle}),\Delta^{(I)}_{*}M^{\langle q\rangle}\bigg).

Evidently the image of
\pi_{p,q}^{F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)}=\pi_{p,q}^{P^{\rm ch}(M)}|_{F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)}:F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\to P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
is precisely this subset, so the map factors as claimed.
∎

The preceding lemma on the compatibility of the grading and filtration provides the following essential corollary:

Corollary 7.17
.

The grading on
P^{\rm ch}(M)
of Equation
7.10
induces a grading

F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}\ ,

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.
