The bottom arrow identifies with the identity map under the direct sum decomposition of Equation
7.10
, so that the lower composition is injective. Commutativity of the diagram implies the same must be true of the upper composition, and thus in particular the upper horizontal arrow is injective. As remarked in the proof of Lemma
7.16
, each of the maps
\pi_{p,q}^{F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)}:F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\to P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
is surjective, and thus so is their direct sum, establishing the desired isomorphism.
∎

We can now complete the proof of Proposition
7.15
:

Proof of Proposition
7.15
.

For each
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the induced special filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}
is bounded and thus admits a splitting, that is, a grading

P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\qquad\text{such that}\qquad F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}=\bigoplus_{i\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\ .

Then by the preceding Corollary
7.17
, we obtain

F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{k}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q\rangle}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigoplus_{i\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}=\bigoplus_{i\leq k}\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\ .

Thus, we obtain that

(7.13)

P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle i\rangle}\qquad\text{for}\qquad P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle i\rangle}=\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\ ,

which thus provides a splitting of the special filtration
F_{\bullet}^{\textup{sp}}P^{\rm ch}(M)
.

Note that the joint filtration can also be described as:

\displaystyle F_{k}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}(F_{\ell}^{M}\cap F_{k-\ell}^{\textup{sp}})P^{\rm ch}(M)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\bigoplus_{p,q\in\mathbb{Z}}\bigoplus_{i\leq\ell}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\right)\cap\left(\bigoplus_{p-q\leq k-\ell}\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{p,q,i\in R(k)}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\ ,

where
R(k)
is the set of triples
p,q,i\in\mathbb{Z}
such that there exists
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying

i\leq\ell\qquad\text{and}\qquad q-p\leq k-\ell\ .

For a fixed
i
, one has the most choices for
p
and
q
if
\ell=i
— since this subtracts the least possible amount from the upper bound on
q-p
. Thus,
R(k)
is simply the set of triples of integers satisfying

q-p\leq k-i\qquad\text{or equivalently}\qquad q-p+i\leq k\ ,

so that we obtain the desired result

F_{k}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{q-p+i\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}(n)\ .

The decomposition of Equation
7.13
thus induces the desired splitting

(7.14)

P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle k\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}\qquad\text{where}\qquad P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle k\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,p,q\\
q-p+i=k\end{subarray}}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\ ,

that is, we have

F_{k}^{M,{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{i\leq k}P^{\rm ch}(M)^{\langle i\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}\ .

∎

7.2.6.

Recall that the classical operad has an auxiliary grading defined as in Section
3.5
. When the D-module
M
is graded, one can refine the grading on
P^{c}(M)
to a trigrading

P^{c}(M)=\bigoplus_{p,q,i}P^{c}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\penalty\ ,

where

(7.15)

P^{c}(M)^{\langle{p,q,i\rangle}}(I)=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{|I|}\\
p_{1}+p_{2}+\dots+p_{|I|}=p\end{subarray}}P^{c}\big(\{M^{\langle p_{1}\rangle},M^{\langle p_{2}\rangle},\dots,M^{\langle p_{|I|}\rangle}\},M^{\langle q\rangle}\big)^{\langle i\rangle}\penalty\ .

We can coarsen this to a single
joint
grading, analogous to the joint filtration on
P^{\rm ch}(M)
:

(7.16)

P^{c}(M)=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}P^{c}(M)^{\langle k\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{ where }\qquad P^{c}(M)^{\langle k\rangle^{\textup{sp}}}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p,q,i\\
q-p+i=k\end{subarray}}P^{c}(M)^{\langle p,q,i\rangle}\penalty\ .

We now prove the generalisation of Theorem
7.13
to the case of the joint filtration.

Theorem 7.18
.

Let
(M,F^{M})
be a filtered, unital-projective D-module which is split in the sense of (
7.6
), then we have an isomorphism of graded operads

\textup{gr}_{F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\xrightarrow{\sim}P^{c}(\textup{gr}_{F^{M}}M)\penalty\ ,

where
P^{c}(\textup{gr}_{F^{M}}M)
is equipped with the grading from (
7.16
).

Proof.

Recall from the proof of Proposition
7.15
that the grading induced by
M
is compatible with the splitting of the special filtration of
M
—in particular, (
7.13
) implies that the associated graded with respect to the special filtration inherits a trigrading

\textup{gr}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{p,q,i\in\mathbb{Z}}(\textup{gr}^{i}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M))^{\langle p,q\rangle}\penalty\ ,

where

(\textup{gr}^{i}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M))^{\langle p,q\rangle}(I)=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{|I|}\\
p_{1}+p_{2}+\dots+p_{|I|}=p\end{subarray}}\textup{gr}^{i}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}\big(\{M^{\langle p_{1}\rangle},M^{\langle p_{2}\rangle},\dots,M^{\langle p_{|I|}\rangle}\},M^{\langle q\rangle}\big)\penalty\ .

Moreover, (
7.14
) implies that we have the identification

\textup{gr}^{k}_{F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=\bigoplus_{q-p+i=k}(\textup{gr}^{i}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M))^{\langle p,q\rangle}\penalty\ .

But from
7.13
,
\textup{gr}^{i}_{F^{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)=P^{c}(M)^{\langle i\rangle}
, and so—at least at the level of trigraded
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules—one has an isomorphism

\textup{gr}_{F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)\cong P^{c}(M)\penalty\ .

That this is an isomorphism of operads follows from the fact that the identification of Theorem
7.13
respects the compositions.
∎

7.2.7.

The joint filtration on
P^{\rm ch}(M)
induces a filtration on
\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{\rm ch}(M)}
with

F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}_{k}\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{\rm ch}(M)}=\Hom_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}}}({\textup{Lie}}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}},F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}_{k}P^{\rm ch}(M))\penalty\ .

Proposition 7.19
.

Let
(M,F^{M})
be a split, filtered D-module, then

\textup{gr}_{F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}}(\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{\rm ch}(M)})=\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{c}(\textup{gr}_{F^{M}}M)}\penalty\ .

Moreover, if
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Lie}}(P^{\rm ch}(M))
is a chiral algebra structure on
M
such that it induces the coisson algebra structure
\overline{\varphi}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(\textup{gr}M))
on
\textup{gr}M
. Then we have an isomorphism of DG Lie algebras

\textup{gr}_{F^{M,{\textup{sp}}}}\ \mathfrak{g}^{\varphi}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{\rm ch}(M)}\cong\mathfrak{g}^{\overline{\varphi}}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{c}(\textup{gr}_{F^{M}}M)}\penalty\ .

Proof.

Since
{{\mathfrak{S}}}{\textup{-Mod}}
is semisimple,
\textup{gr}_{F^{M},{\textup{sp}}}\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{\rm ch}(M)}\cong\mathfrak{g}_{{\textup{Lie}},\textup{gr}_{F^{M},{\textup{sp}}}P^{\rm ch}(M)}
, whereupon the result follows from Theorem
7.18
, noting that the second result follows since the isomorphism of
loc. cit.
is an isomorphism of operads.
∎

Proposition 7.20
.

Let
(M,F^{M})
be a split, filtered D-module, and suppose
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\textup{Lie}}(P^{\rm ch}(M))
is a chiral algebra structure on
M
such that it induces the coisson algebra structure
\overline{\varphi}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(\textup{gr}M))
on
\textup{gr}M
.

Then we have the following inequality

{\rm dim}\ H^{i}_{\rm ch}(M,\varphi)\leq{\rm dim}\ H^{i}_{c}(\textup{gr}M,\overline{\varphi})\penalty\ .

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
7.19
, using the usual spectral sequence on a filtered complex.
∎

7.2.8.

Write
D_{\hbar}(X){\textup{-Mod}}\cong D(X)\boxtimes_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]{\textup{-Mod}}
for the category of families of D-modules over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
. Likewise, we write
D_{\hbar}(X)^{\textup{gr}}
for the category of families of D-modules over the stack
[\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}/\mathbb{G}_{\hbar}]
.

We say that
M_{\hbar}\in D_{\hbar}(X)
is
trivializable
if there exists a trivialization:

M_{\hbar}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ ,

where
M_{0}\cong M\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{D}}(X)
. Similarly,
M_{\hbar}\in D_{\hbar}(X)
is said to be trivializable if there exits a graded trivialization
M_{\hbar}\xrightarrow{\sim}M_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
.

The trivializable
D_{\hbar}(X)
-modules are equivalent to ordinary D-modules by taking the central fibre. Moreover, the graded, trivializable
D_{\hbar}(X)
modules are equivalent to the split, filtered D-modules by

M_{\hbar}\mapsto(M_{0},F^{M_{0}})

where
M_{0}=M\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}
the central fibre and
F^{M_{0}}
is the split filtration induced by the grading on
M_{0}
. The inverse is

M_{0}\mapsto{\textup{Rees}}_{\hbar}^{F^{M_{0}}}(M_{0})\penalty\ .

Indeed, viewing
M_{\hbar}\in D_{\hbar}(X)
as a trivially graded
D_{\hbar}(X)
-module, the corresponding D-module
M_{0}
is trivially filtered.

Thus, for any
M_{\hbar}\in D_{\hbar}(X))
or
M_{\hbar}\in D_{\hbar}(X)^{\textup{gr}}
we may define the
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]
-operad

P_{\hbar}^{\rm ch}(M_{\hbar})\coloneqq{\textup{Rees}}^{F^{M_{0},{\textup{sp}}}}_{\hbar}(P^{\rm ch}(M_{0}))\penalty\ ,

where the filtration on
M_{0}
is taken to be trivial if
M_{\hbar}
is not graded. Since the filtration on
M_{0}
is split in both cases, by Proposition
7.15
, the filtration on
P^{\rm ch}(M_{0})
is split too and there exists a trivialization of
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules,

P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(M_{\hbar})\xrightarrow{\sim}(P_{\hbar}^{\rm ch}(M_{\hbar}))_{0}\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\cong P^{c}(M_{0})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ ,

where we used Theorem
7.18
to identify the central fibre with the classical operad.

Like in Section
6.4
we can consider the completed version of the above construction. Let
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)=D(X)\widehat{\boxtimes}_{\mathbb{K}}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
be the category of families of D-modules over the formal neighbourhood of the origin of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\hbar}
, and write
D_{\hat{\hbar}}^{\textup{gr}}(X)
for it graded analogue. We can define the notion of a trivializable
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-module and
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-module analogously.

Every trivializable
M_{\hat{\hbar}}\in D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)^{\textup{gr}}
defines a split, filtered D-module
(M_{0},F^{M_{0}})
, with filtration induced by the grading on
M_{0}
and

M_{\hat{\hbar}}=\widehat{{\textup{Rees}}}_{\hbar}^{F^{M_{0}}}(M_{0})={\textup{Rees}}_{\hbar}^{F^{M_{0}}}(M_{0})\widehat{\otimes}_{{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\penalty\ .

Once again, for
M_{\hat{\hbar}}\in D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
the corresponding
M_{0}
is trivially filtered.

Given a trivializable
M_{\hat{\hbar}}\in D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X))
or
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)^{\textup{gr}}
we define the
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
-operad

P_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hbar})=\widehat{{\textup{Rees}}}_{\hbar}^{F^{M_{0},{\textup{sp}}}}(P^{\rm ch}(M_{0}))\penalty\ .

As before, the splitting of the joint filtration induces a trivialization

P_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hat{\hbar}}\xrightarrow{\sim}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hat{\hbar}}))_{0}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\cong P^{c}(M_{0})\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\penalty\ .

Remark 7.21
.

The data of
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hat{\hbar}}))
(or its graded counterpart
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hat{\hbar}}))
corresponds to a (graded) chiral algebra structure on
M_{\hat{\hbar}}
such that its restriction to the central fibre,
\varphi_{0}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(M_{0}))
, defines a (graded) coisson algebra structure on
M_{0}
.

Remark 7.22
.

Let
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(M_{\hat{\hbar}}))
, by way of the trivialization of (
7.14
) we can think of
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}
as a pair

m_{\hat{\hbar}}\oplus b_{\hat{\hbar}}\in P^{c}(M_{0})(2)\otimes{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}=\Hom_{D(X)}(M_{0}\otimes^{!}M_{0},M_{0})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\oplus\Hom_{D(X^{2})}(M_{0}\boxtimes M_{0},\Delta^{(2)}_{*}M_{0})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}\penalty\ .

Restricted to the central fibre,
m_{0}:M_{0}\otimes^{!}M_{0}\rightarrow M_{0}
is a commutative product and
b_{0}:M_{0}\boxtimes M_{0}\to\Delta^{(2)}_{*}M_{0}
is a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
-bracket on
M_{0}
compatible with
m_{0}
in the sense that
(M_{0},m_{0},b_{0})
is a coisson algebra.

7.2.9.

We set
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
and work in the weakly translation invariant setting. We can identify the weakly translation invariant
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-modules with
{\mathbb{K}}[{{\partial}}]{\textup{-Mod}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}{\textup{-Mod}})
. In this setting, following Section
4.2
, one recovers the notion of quantization of a vertex Poisson algebra as introduced in
[
AKM15
]
. Let us explore this in some detail.

Given a trivializable
V_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\mathbb{K}}[{{\partial}}]{\textup{-Mod}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}{\textup{-Mod}})
, we can make sense of
P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})
by way of the Rees construction—as we did before.

We have an isomorphism of the central fibres,

P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{0})\cong P^{\mathbb{G}_{a},c}(V_{0})\penalty\ ,

and a trivialization of
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-modules,

P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hbar}(V_{\hbar})\xrightarrow{\sim}P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{0}(V_{0})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\cong P^{c,\mathbb{G}_{a}}(V_{0})\otimes{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]\penalty\ .

Concretely,
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{\hat{\hbar}}))
corresponds to an integral
\lambda
-bracket
I_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}:V_{\hat{\hbar}}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}V_{\hat{\hbar}}\to V_{\hat{\hbar}}[\lambda]
with the property that

I_{0,\lambda}:V_{0}\otimes V_{0}\to V_{0}

is a commutative multiplication on
V_{0}
and

\lim_{\hbar\to 0}\frac{1}{\hbar}\tfrac{dI_{{\hat{\hbar}},\lambda}}{d\lambda}:V_{0}\otimes V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0}[\lambda]

defines a
\lambda
-bracket on
V_{0}
compatible with the commutative multiplication—making
V_{0}
a vertex Poisson algebra. To summarize:

Proposition 7.23
.

The data of
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar}}(P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{\hat{\hbar}})
is an almost commutative
\hbar
-adic vertex algebra structure on
V_{\hbar}
that quantizes—in the sense of Definition
4.23
—the central fibre: the vertex Poisson algebra
(V_{0},\varphi_{0})
.

Similarly,
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar}}(P^{{\rm ch},\mathbb{G}_{a}}_{\hat{\hbar}}(V_{\hat{\hbar}}))
is a graded quantization of the graded vertex Poisson algebra
(V_{0},\varphi_{0})
.

7.3.
Quantization of coisson algebras

Let
R\in D(X)
be a unital-projective D-module and
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
, so that
(R,\varphi)
is a coisson algebra.

In light of Remarks
7.21
and
7.22
, we make the following definition

Definition 7.24
.

Given a coisson algebra
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
, a
quantization
,
(R_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
, is a

(i)

trivializable
R_{\hat{\hbar}}\in D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
with an isomorphism of the central fibre
R_{0}=R_{\hbar}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}}{\mathbb{K}}_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}R

(ii)

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(R_{\hat{\hbar}}))

such that the isomorphism
R_{0}\cong R
extends to an isomorphism of coisson algebras
(R_{0},\varphi_{0})\cong(R,\varphi)
.

If
(R,\varphi)
is a graded coisson algebra, then we say that a quantization
(R_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
is
graded
if
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(R_{\hat{\hbar}}))
and
(R_{0},\varphi_{0})\cong(R,\varphi)
as graded coisson algebras.

7.3.1.

Fix
R_{\hat{\hbar}}
to be the trivial family,
R\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
. Consider a deformation of
\varphi
,

\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(R_{\hbar})}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})\penalty\ .

By construction,
R_{\hat{\hbar}}
is a trivializable
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-module with central fibre
equal
to
R
and
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hat{\hbar}}}(P^{\rm ch}_{\hat{\hbar}}(R_{\hat{\hbar}}))
is such that
\varphi_{0}=\varphi
. Thus,
(R_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
is a quantization of the coisson algebra
(R,\varphi)
. Indeed, the scheme of deformations parameterises all quantizations of the coisson algebra
(R,\varphi)
whose underlying
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
module is our fixed
R_{\hbar}
.

Let
S
be the Artinian local ring,
{\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m}
, or the complete local ring
{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
.
Therefore, we make the following definitions:

(7.17)

{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)={\textup{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P_{\hbar}^{\rm ch}(R_{\hbar})}(S)\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}(S)={\textup{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(R_{\hbar})}(S)\penalty\ ,

of the schemes of quantizations and graded quantizations of
(\varphi_{0},R_{0})
whose underlying (graded)
D_{\hbar}(X)
-module is the trivial family.

Similarly, we define the stack of quantizations and graded quantizations as

(7.18)

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)={\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(R_{\hbar})}(S)\penalty\ ,\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}(S)={\mathfrak{Def}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(R_{\hbar})}(S).

The following remark is mirror to Remark
7.4
.

Remark 7.25
.

The stacks
{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}(S)
and
{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}(S)
actually parameterise the isomorphism classes of all (graded) quantizations of
(R,\varphi)
. This is because the underlying
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-module of every quantization is isomorphic to
R\widehat{\otimes}{\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]}
and so every quantization of
(R,\varphi)
has a representative whose underlying
D_{\hat{\hbar}}(X)
-module is trivial

One may think of our scheme of quantizations as a kind of partial gauge fixing of a larger, more glorious, scheme of quantizations, which in addition parameterizes the data of the central fibre isomorphism.

7.3.2.

We can now apply the results of Section
6
to the chiral setting.

Theorem 7.26
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
be a coisson algebra and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
a quantization defined modulo
\hbar^{m}
. Then there exists an extension

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\ \in{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}\varphi({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P_{\hbar}^{\rm ch}(R_{\hbar})}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2}_{c}(\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi}^{\rm ch}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1}_{c}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Theorem
6.14
to the present setting; the operad
{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar}
is Koszul, the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-module of
{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar}
is evidently canonically trivialized, and the trivialization of the underlying
{{\mathfrak{S}}}
-module of
P^{\rm ch}_{\hbar}(R_{\hbar})
is given by that of Proposition
7.15
.
∎

Corollary 7.27
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
a coisson algebra such that
H^{2}_{c}(\varphi)=0
. Then
{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is nonempty.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.20
to the present setting.
∎

Corollary 7.28
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
be a coisson algebra such that
H^{1}_{c}(\varphi)=0
. Then, if there exists
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, every quantization of
(R,\varphi)
is isomorphic to
(R_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.21
to the present setting.
∎

Theorem 7.29
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
a graded coisson algebra and
\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{\rm ch}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})
a graded quantization defined modulo
\hbar^{m}
. Then there exists an extension

\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m+1}}\ \in{\textup{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{\rm ch}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}

if and only if the cohomology class of the obstruction vanishes, that is,

[{\textup{Obs}}^{{\textup{Lie}}_{\hbar},P_{\hbar}^{{\rm ch}}(R_{\hbar})}_{m}(\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}})]=0\ \in H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)\ .

Further, when the obstruction class vanishes, the space of such extensions up to isomorphism,

{\mathfrak{Quant}}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}^{\rm ch}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m+1})\times_{{\mathfrak{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}[\hbar]/\hbar^{m})}\{\varphi_{\hbar/\hbar^{m}}\}\ ,

is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)
, the
-m^{th}
graded component of
H^{1}_{c}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Theorem
6.16
to the present setting, noting the required hypotheses hold as in the proof of Theorem
7.26
.
∎

Corollary 7.30
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}^{\textup{gr}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
a graded coisson algebra such that
H^{2,\langle-m\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)=0
for all
m>0
. Then
{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is nonempty.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.22
to the present setting.
∎

Corollary 7.31
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
a graded coisson algebra such that
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)=0
for all
m>0
. Then, if there exists
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
every graded quantization of
(R,\varphi)
is isomorphic to
(R_{\hat{\hbar}},\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}})
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.23
to the present setting.
∎

Corollary 7.32
.

Let
\varphi\in{\textup{Alg}}_{{\textup{Lie}}}(P^{c}(R))
a graded coisson algebra such that
H^{1,\langle-m\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)=\{0\}
for all
m\neq m_{0}
,
m_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, if there exists a graded quantization
\varphi_{\hat{\hbar}}\in{\textup{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
, the stack of graded quantizations
{\mathfrak{Quant}}^{\rm ch}_{\varphi,{\textup{gr}}}({\mathbb{K}}{[\![\hbar]\!]})
is a torsor for
H^{1,\langle-m_{0}\rangle}_{c}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

This follows from applying Corollary
6.24
to the present setting.
∎

8.
Variational Poisson cohomology of jets of maps to affine symplectic varieties

8.1.
Overview of main result on variational Poisson cohomology

The goal of this section is to prove the following theorem, which is one of the main results of the present work:

Let
Y
be an affine symplectic variety,
\mathcal{Y}=\mathcal{J}Y
the corresponding affine D-scheme of jets of maps to
Y
and
R=(R,m)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{J}Y}
the corresponding
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}(X)
algebra. The symplectic structure
\omega_{Y}
on
Y
induces a coisson structure
b_{\omega_{Y}}
on
R
, and by mild abuse of notation we also let
R=(R,m,b_{\omega_{Y}})
denote the resulting coisson algebra. Let
C^{\bullet}_{c}(R)=C^{\bullet}_{c}(R,m,b_{\omega_{Y}})
denote the classical cochains on
R
from Proposition
5.38
. The main result of this section is the following identification of the cohomology groups
H^{\bullet}_{c}(R)=H_{{\textup{Lie}},P^{c}(R)}^{\bullet}(\varphi)
for
\varphi:{\textup{Lie}}\to P^{c}(R)
the corresponding map of operads.

Theorem 8.1
.

For
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
, there is a natural isomorphism

H_{c}^{k}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{J}Y})\cong H^{k+2}_{\textup{dR}}(Y)

for
k\geq 1
.

Conceptually, the proof is essentially identical to that of the analogous fact for affine symplectic varieties, which was the content of Theorem
7.8
. We defer the analogous discussion of the implications for the deformation theory of coisson algebras to Section
9.1
below.

The formal proof is given in Section
8.2
, with each of the main ingredients proved in the following sections: In Section
8.3
, we introduce the requisite internal
\Hom
objects for the categories
{D}(X)
and
{D}(R)
, as well as for their spaces of multi-linear maps with respect to the various pseudo-tensor category structures used in the proof. In Section
8.4
, we show that the middle de Rham cohomology of the internal analogue of the variational cochains recovers the global variational cochains, which by Corollary
5.45
computes the cohomology of the classical cochains as on the right hand side of the isomorphism in Theorem
8.1
. In Section
8.5
, we show that the internal variational cochains admit a natural isomorphism of complexes with the internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid
\Omega^{1}_{R}
determined by the coisson structure on
R
, and moreover that under our symplectic hypothesis on
Y
, this admits a further isomorphism of complexes to the truncated relative de Rham complex of
\mathcal{Y}\to X
. Finally, in Section
8.6
we show that the corresponding middle de Rham cohomology of the truncated relative de Rham complex is isomorphic in the derived category to the truncated de Rham complex of
Y
.

8.2.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

The proof amounts, essentially, to combining the main results of the sections which follow below.

Let
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}
,
Y
an affine symplectic variety and
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{J}}Y
. Since
Y
is smooth,
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{J}}Y
is very smooth and by Corollary
5.45
, the inclusion
C_{{{\textup{PV}}}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}Y}){\ \hookrightarrow\ }C_{c}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}Y})
is a quasi-isomorphism. We have a chain of isomorphisms

C^{\bullet}_{{{\textup{PV}}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}Y})\cong h\big({\mathcal{C}}_{{{\textup{PV}}}}^{\bullet}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}Y})\big)\cong h\big(\Omega^{\bullet\geq 1}_{\mathcal{Y}/X}\big)[1]\penalty\ ,

where the first isomorphism follows from
8.36
and the second from Proposition
8.43
.

Now, by Proposition
8.47
, we have an isomorphism in cohomology
H^{k}(h(\Omega^{\bullet\geq 1}_{\mathcal{Y}/X}))\cong H^{k+1}_{{\textup{dR}}}(Y)
for
k\geq 2
. Combining, we have the desired result for
k\geq 1

H^{k}_{c}({\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}Y})\cong H^{k+2}_{\textup{dR}}(Y)\penalty\ .

\Box

8.3.
Internal Hom objects for D-modules and quasicoherent sheaves on D-schemes

There are natural internal Hom objects for the derived category
{\textup{D}}(X)
:

Definition 8.2
.

The naive internal Hom object
{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\in{\textup{D}}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
for the category
{\textup{D}}(X)
is defined by

{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)(U)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(U)^{!}}(j^{!}L,j^{!}M)(U)

on any open set
U\subset X
, where
j:U\to X
the inclusion.

Let
{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(X)\subset{\textup{D}}(X)
denote the full subcategory of objects of
{\textup{D}}(X)
with
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-coherent cohomology objects, and let
L\in{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
and
M\in{\textup{D}}(X)
.

Definition 8.3
.

The (genuine) internal Hom object
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\in{\textup{D}}(X)
is defined by

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}^{r}(X)}(L^{r},M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

This satisfies the following relation with the usual Hom space in
{D}(X)
:

Proposition 8.4
.

There exists a natural quasi-isomorphism

\pi_{*}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\xrightarrow{\cong}\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\ .

Definition 8.5
.

The duality functor
{{\mathbb{D}}}:{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(X)\to{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
is defined by

{{\mathbb{D}}}M={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(M,\omega_{X})={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}^{r}(X)}(M^{r},\omega_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

This satisfies the following relation with the genuine internal Hom functor

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
L\in{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
and
M\in{\textup{D}}(X)
. There is a natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\cong({{\mathbb{D}}}L)\otimes^{\mathbb{L},!}M\ .

8.3.1.

There are also analogues of the above internal Hom objects in the abelian category
{D}(X)
:

Definition 8.7
.

The naive internal Hom object
{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\in{\textup{D}}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
for the category
{\textup{D}}(X)
is defined by

{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)(U)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(U)^{!}}(j^{!,\heartsuit}L,j^{!,\heartsuit}M)(U)

on any open set
U\subset X
, where
j:U\to X
the inclusion.

Let
{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)\subset{D}(X)
denote the full subcategory on coherent D-modules, and let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
and
M\in{D}(X)
.

Definition 8.8
.

The (genuine) internal Hom object
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M)\in{D}(X)
is defined by

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}^{r}(X)}(L^{r},M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ \in{D}^{r}(X)\ .

We also introduce the following abelian variant of the duality functor:

Definition 8.9
.

The duality functor
(\cdot)^{\circ}:{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)\to{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
is defined by

M^{\circ}={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(M,\Omega^{d_{X}}_{X})={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}^{r}(X)}(M^{r},\Omega^{d_{X}}_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

Note that if
M\in{D}(X)
is locally projective then so is
M^{\circ}
, and we have that
M^{\circ}\cong{{\mathbb{D}}}M[-d_{X}]
.

This satisfies the following analogous relation with the genuine internal Hom functor

Proposition 8.10
.

Let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
and
M\in{D}(X)
. There is a natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M)\cong L^{\circ}\otimes^{!}M\ .

Although it will not in general be true that the plain internal Hom object correctly computes the space of Homs in the abelian category, we have the following:

Proposition 8.11
.

Let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
be locally projective and
M\in{D}(X)
. Then

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(X)}(L,M)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M)\ ,

and if in addition
X
is affine and
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M)
is locally projective, we have

h({\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(L,M))\xrightarrow{\cong}\Hom_{{D}(X)}(L,M)\ .

8.3.2.

Let
L_{i}\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(X)
and
M\in{D}(X)
. We also have the following natural variants internal to
{D}(X)
of the spaces of multilinear operations for the various pseudo-tensor structures on
{D}(X)
:

Definition 8.12
.

The internal operation spaces for
{D}(X)^{!}
are defined by

\mathcal{P}^{!}(\{L_{i}\},M)={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(\otimes^{!}_{i\in I}L_{i},M)\ .

Definition 8.13
.

The internal operation spaces for
{D}(X)^{*}
are defined by

\mathcal{P}^{*}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\Delta^{I!}_{X}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X^{I})}(\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i},\Delta^{I}_{*}M)

Definition 8.14
.

The internal operation spaces for
{D}(X)^{c}
are defined by

(8.1)

\mathcal{P}^{c}_{I}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\bigoplus_{(\pi_{S},S)\in Q(I)}\mathcal{P}_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)_{S}

where for each
\pi_{S}:I\to S
we define

\mathcal{P}_{I}^{c}(\{L_{i}\},M)_{S}=\mathcal{P}_{S}^{*}\big(\{\otimes!_{i\in I_{s}}L_{i}\}_{s\in S},M\big)\otimes\big(\otimes_{s\in S}{\textup{Lie}}(|I_{s}|)\big)\ .

We also have the following concrete description of the internal operation spaces for the endomorphism operad of a single object
M\in{D}(X)^{c}
:

{\mathcal{E}nd}_{{D}(X)^{c}}(M)(n)=\mathcal{P}_{n}^{c}(\{M\}_{i=1}^{n},M)=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n-1}{\mathcal{P}}_{n}^{c}(\{M\}_{i=1}^{n},M)^{\langle p\rangle}\penalty\ ,

where the graded components are defined as in Proposition
3.34
by

(8.2)

{\mathcal{P}}_{n}^{c}(\{M\}_{i=1}^{n},M)^{\langle p\rangle}=\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}{\textup{Ind}}_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{1}}\times...\times{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{p+1}}}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\bigg(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{p+1}(\{M^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{p+1},M)\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})\bigg)\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\ .

8.3.3.

There are natural internal Hom objects for the categories
{\textup{D}}(R)
and
{D}(R)
, analogous to those for
{\textup{D}}(X)
and
{D}(X)
defined above:

Definition 8.15
.

The naive internal Hom object
{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\in{\textup{D}}(R{\textup{-Mod}}(\QC(X)))
for the category
{D}(R)
is defined by

{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)(U)=\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(j^{!}R{\textup{-Mod}}({D}(U)^{!}))}(j^{!}L,j^{!}M)(U)

on any open set
U\subset X
, where
j:U\to X
the inclusion.

Let
{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(R)\subset{\textup{D}}(R)
denote the full subcategory of complexes with
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-coherent,
R
-finitely generated cohomology modules, and let
L\in{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
and
M\in{D}(R)
.

Definition 8.16
.

The (genuine) internal Hom object
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\in{\textup{D}}(R)
is defined by

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L^{r},M^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

Note that in the case
R^{\ell,\heartsuit}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
is the unit
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra, these reduce to the usual definitions of the naive and genuine internal Hom objects in the category of D-modules recalled in Section
3.1
.

The internal Hom object satisfies the following relation with the usual Hom spaces in
{\textup{D}}(R)
:

Proposition 8.17
.

There is a natural isomorphism

\pi_{*}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\xrightarrow{\cong}\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\ .

8.3.4.

In particular, we obtain a duality functor on
{\textup{D}}(R)_{\textup{coh}}
:

Definition 8.18
.

The duality functor
{{\mathbb{D}}}_{R}:{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(R)\to{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
is defined by

{{\mathbb{D}}}_{R}M={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(M,R)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{\textup{D}}^{r}(R)}(M^{r},R^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

This satisfies the following relation with the genuine internal Hom functor

Proposition 8.19
.

Let
L\in{\textup{D}}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
and
M\in{\textup{D}}(R)
. There is a natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\cong({{\mathbb{D}}}_{R}L)\otimes^{\mathbb{L},!}_{R}M\ .

We also have the following plain (underived) analogues of the internal Hom functors for
{D}(R)
:

Definition 8.20
.

The naive internal Hom object
{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)\in R{\textup{-Mod}}(\QC(X))
for the category
{D}(R)
is defined by

{\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)(U)=\Hom_{j^{!}R{\textup{-Mod}}({D}(U)^{!})}(j^{!}L,j^{!}M)(U)

on any open set
U\subset X
, where
j:U\to X
the inclusion.

Let
{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)\subset{D}(R)
denote the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-coherent,
R
-finitely generated modules, and let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
and
M\in{D}(R)
.

Definition 8.21
.

The (genuine) internal Hom object
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)\in{D}(R)
is defined by

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}^{r}(R)}(L^{r},M^{r}\otimes^{!}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

Note that in the case
R=\Omega_{X}^{d_{X}}
is the unit
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra, these reduce to the usual definitions of the naive and genuine internal Hom objects in the category of D-modules recalled in Section
3.1
.

8.3.5.

In particular, we obtain a duality functor on
{D}(R)_{\textup{coh}}
:

Definition 8.22
.

The duality functor
(\cdot)^{\circ_{R}}:{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)\to{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
is defined by

M^{\circ_{R}}={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(M,R)={\underline{\textup{Hom}}}_{{D}^{r}(R)}(M^{r},R^{r}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X})\ .

Note that if
M\in{D}(R)
is projective then so is
M^{\circ_{R}}
, and we have that
M^{\circ_{R}}\cong{{\mathbb{D}}}_{R}M[-d_{X}]
.

This satisfies the following relation with the genuine internal Hom functor

Proposition 8.23
.

Let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
and
M\in{D}(R)
. There is a natural isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)\cong L^{\circ_{R}}\otimes^{!}_{R}M\ .

Again, it will not in general be true that the plain internal Hom object correctly computes the space of Homs in the abelian category, we have the following:

Proposition 8.24
.

Let
L\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
be projective and
M\in{D}(R)
. Then

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}(L,M)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)\ ,

and if in addition
X
is affine and
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M)
is locally projective in
{D}(X)
, we have

h({\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(L,M))\cong\Hom_{{D}(R)}(L,M)\ .

8.3.6.

Let
L_{i}\in{D}_{\textup{coh}}(R)
and
M\in{D}(R)
. We also have the following natural variants internal to
{D}(R)
of the spaces of multilinear operations for the various pseudo-tensor structures on
{D}(R)
:

Definition 8.25
.

The internal operation spaces for
{D}(R)^{!}
are defined by

\mathcal{P}^{!}_{I,R}(\{L_{i}\},M)={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(\otimes_{R,i\in I}^{!}L_{i},M)\ .

More explicitly, by Proposition
8.23
we have

\mathcal{P}^{!}_{I,R}(\{L_{i}\},M)\cong(\otimes_{R,i\in I}^{!}L_{i})^{\circ}\otimes^{R,!}M\ .

Definition 8.26
.

The internal operation spaces for
{D}(R)^{*}
are defined by

\mathcal{P}_{I,R}^{*}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\Delta_{R}^{I\bullet}\Delta^{I!}_{X}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes I})}(\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i},\Delta_{*}M)\ .

8.4.
Comparison of global and internal variational cochains

To begin, we introduce the internal analogues of some of the various deformation-obstruction complexes for algebras of the chiral kind:

Definition 8.27
.

For
R=(R,m_{\varphi})
a
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra, we define the internal Harrison and degree
p
higher André–Quillen cochains as the objects in
{\textup{D}}(X)
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(R)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{!}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{Har}}\right)

\displaystyle\in{\textup{D}}(X)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}}(\{{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1};R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}

\displaystyle\in{\textup{D}}(X)

where
d_{\text{Har}}:C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(M,m)\to C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{Har}}}(M,m_{\varphi})[1]
the Harrison differential determined by
m
, internal to the monoidal category
{D}(X)^{!}
enriched over
{D}(X)
, and
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}}
denotes the derived analogue of the internal Hom object introduced in Definition
8.26
, defined for
L_{i},M\in{D}(R)
by

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}}(\{L_{i}\},M)=\mathbb{L}\Delta_{R}^{I\bullet}\circ\Delta^{I!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes I})}(\boxtimes_{i\in I}L_{i},\Delta_{*}M)\ .

Similarly, for
L=(R,b)
a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra, we define the internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{CE}(R,b)=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{*}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\text{CE}}\right)\qquad\in{\textup{D}}(X)\ ,

for
d_{\text{CE}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}}}(R,b)\to C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}}}(R,b)[1]
the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
b
, internal to the pseudo-tensor category
{D}(X)^{*}
.

We the following analogue of Corollary
5.36
also follows from Proposition
5.34
:

Corollary 8.28
.

Let
R
be a very smooth
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra. Then
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)
is acyclic in non-zero degree, and

\mathcal{H}^{0}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(\Omega^{1}_{R},R)=\Theta_{R}\ ,

the tangent module
\Theta_{R}\in{D}(R)
.

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
,
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)
is acyclic except in cohomological degree
p
, and

\mathcal{H}^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)^{*}}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\cong\wedge^{p+1}_{R}\Theta_{R}\ .

the module of degree
{p+1}
polyvector fields
\wedge^{p+1}_{R}\Theta_{R}\in{D}(R)
.

Throughout the remainder of this section, let
R=(R,m,b)
be a coisson algebra. Following Proposition
5.38
, we make the following definition:

Definition 8.29
.

The internal classical cochains on
R
are defined by

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c}(R)=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\textup{CE}}\right)\qquad\in{\textup{D}}(X)\ ,

where
d_{\textup{CE}}:\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c}(R)\to\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c}(R)[1]
is the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by
\mu=(m,b)\in P_{2}^{c}(\{R,R\},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{2}}

In analogy with Proposition
5.39
, we have:

Proposition 8.30
.

There is a natural bicomplex
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{c}(R)=\oplus_{p,q=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}^{p,q}_{c}(R)
of objects in
{D}(X)
defined by

\mathcal{C}^{p,q}_{c}(R)=\mathcal{C}^{p,q}_{c}(R,m,b)=\mathcal{P}_{p+q+1}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{p+q+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+q+1}}^{\langle p\rangle}\qquad d_{h}=d_{b}:\mathcal{C}^{p,q}_{c}\to\mathcal{C}^{p+1,q}_{c}\qquad d_{v}=d_{m}:\mathcal{C}^{p,q}_{c}\to\mathcal{C}^{p,q+1}_{c}

such that the total complex is naturally isomorphic to the internal classical cochains, that is,

\textup{Tot }\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\bullet}_{c}(R)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\bullet}(R)\ .

Proof.

The proof is precisely the same as that of Proposition
5.39
,
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

The first non-zero row and column define quotient complexes in
{\textup{D}}(X)

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c,m}(R)=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}^{\langle 0\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c,b}(R)=(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}^{\langle n-1\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{b})\ .

We have the following analogue of Proposition
5.40
:

Proposition 8.31
.

There are natural identifications of complexes

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c,m}(R)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\textup{Har}}(R,m)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{c,b}(R)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(R,b)\ .

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of Proposition
5.40
,
mutatis mutandis
.
∎

More generally, for each
p\geq 0
the
p^{th}
row of the above bicomplex defines a complex

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R)=\left(\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}^{\langle p\rangle}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{m}\ \right)\qquad\in{\textup{D}}(X)

and we have the following analogue of Proposition
5.26
:

Proposition 8.32
.

For each
p\geq 1
, there are natural identifications of complexes

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R)[p]\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)\ ,

for
R=(M,m)
. In particular, we have
\mathcal{H}^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)^{*}}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}={\textup{Comm}}
and
\mathcal{D}={D}(X)^{!}
considered as a
{\mathbb{K}}
-linear symmetric monoidal category, enriched over the
{\mathbb{K}}
-linear category
{D}(X)
, so that the
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra
R=(R,m)\in{\textup{Alg}}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{D})
. By Theorem 12.4.3 of
[
LV12
]
internal to
\mathcal{D}
, the object
{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\in R{\textup{-Mod}}({\textup{D}}(X)^{!})={\textup{D}}(R)
admits a canonical representative

{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\cong\left(R\otimes^{!}\mathcal{P}^{\textup{\mbox{!`}}}(R)\ ,\ d_{\pi_{k}}\right)=\left(R\otimes^{!}\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\textup{Lie}}(n)^{*}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}R^{\otimes^{!}n}[n-1]\ ,\ d^{{\textup{Har}}}\ \right)\ ,

where
d^{{\textup{Har}}}
denotes the differential on Harrison chains on
R
internal to
{D}(X)
, as follows from Lemma 12.4.4 and Proposition 12.4.5 of
loc. cit.
. In the case
p=1
, we use this to calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)}({\mathbb{L}}_{R},R)

\displaystyle\cong\left({\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R)}(R\otimes^{!}\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\textup{Lie}}(n)^{*}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}R^{\otimes^{!}n}[n-1],R)\ ,\ d_{{\textup{Har}}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}({\textup{Lie}}(n)^{*}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}R^{\otimes^{!}n},R)[1-n]\ ,\ d_{{\textup{Har}}}\right)

\displaystyle\cong\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X)}(R^{\otimes^{!}n},R)^{{\textup{Sh}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{{\textup{Har}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}_{\textup{Har}}^{\bullet}(R)

,

where
d_{\textup{Har}}=(d^{\textup{Har}})^{*}
denotes the differential on Harrison cochains on
R
internal to
{D}(X)
, the first isomorphism follows from the fact our chosen representative is a complex of projective objects of
{D}(R)
, the second follows from the induction adjunction, and the third follows from the proof of Proposition 13.1.4 of
[
LV12
]
, recalled above as Proposition
5.17
.

More generally, for
p\geq 0
we can analogously calculate the representative of
{\mathbb{L}}_{R}^{\boxtimes p+1}\in{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes I})
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{L}}_{R}^{\boxtimes p+1}

\displaystyle\cong\left(\ \left(R\otimes^{!}\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}{\textup{Lie}}(n)^{*}\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}]}R^{\otimes^{!}n}[n-1]\right)^{\boxtimes p+1}\ ,\ d^{\textup{Har}}_{\textup{Tot},\langle p\rangle}\ \right)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}R^{\boxtimes p+1}\otimes^{!}\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}\boxtimes_{i=1}^{p+1}R^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\right)\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\prod_{i=1}^{p+1}{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{i}}]}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})^{*}[n-p-1]\ ,\ d^{\textup{Har}}_{\textup{Tot},\langle p\rangle}\ \right)

and thus analogously compute the degree
p
higher André–Quillen cochains as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{\textup{AQ}}(R)={\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R)^{*}}(\{{\mathbb{L}}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1};R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{L}\Delta_{R}^{I\bullet}\Delta^{(p+1)!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p+1})}({\mathbb{L}}_{R}^{\boxtimes p+1},\Delta^{(p+1)}_{*}R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}

\displaystyle\hskip-48.36958pt\cong\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}\Delta_{R}^{I\bullet}\Delta^{(p+1)!}_{X}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p+1})}\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}R^{\boxtimes p+1}\otimes^{!}\left(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{p+1}R^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\right)\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\prod_{i=1}^{p+1}{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{i}}]}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})^{*}[n-p-1],\Delta^{(p+1)}_{*}R\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}\Delta_{R}^{I\bullet}\Delta^{(p+1)!}_{X}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(X^{\boxtimes p+1})}\left(\boxtimes_{i=1}^{p+1}R^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}},\Delta^{(p+1)}_{*}R\right)\otimes_{{\mathbb{K}}[\prod_{i=1}^{p+1}{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{i}}]}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})[1-n+p]\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}{\textup{Ind}}_{\prod_{i=1}^{p+1}{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{i}}}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{p+1}\left(\{R^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{p+1},R\right)\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}[1-n+p]

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}\left(\left(\bigoplus_{n_{1}+...+n_{p+1}=n}{\textup{Ind}}_{\prod_{i=1}^{p+1}{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n_{i}}}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}\left(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{p+1}\left(\{R^{\otimes^{!}n_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{p+1},R\right)\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{p+1}{\textup{Lie}}(n_{i})\right)\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}\right)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n+p]

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{n=p+1}^{\infty}{\mathcal{P}}_{n}^{c}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)^{\langle p\rangle}_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n+p]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R)[p]

where we have equipped the cohomologically graded vector spaces given above following the initial isomorphism with
d_{\textup{Har}}^{\textup{Tot},\langle p\rangle}=(d^{\textup{Har}}_{\textup{Tot},\langle p\rangle})^{*}
, which identifies with the differential
d_{m}
on
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R)
as claimed in the final equality, again by Lemma 12.4.4 of
[
LV12
]
. The final, non-trivial isomorphism follows from the expression in Equation
8.2
.

∎

We now give the internal analogue of Definition
5.43
and Corollary
5.44
:

Definition 8.33
.

The (reduced) internal variational Poisson cochains on
R
is the subcomplex of
\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\bullet}(R)
given by

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R)=\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{p,\langle p\rangle}_{c,m}(R,m_{\varphi})[-p]\ ,\ d_{b}\ \right)\qquad\in{\textup{D}}(X).

Corollary 8.34
.

The internal variational Poisson cochains on
R
admits an isomorphism of complexes

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R)\cong\left(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p+1}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{k=1}^{p+1};R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p+1}}[-p]\ ,\ \tilde{d}_{b}\ \right)\ ,

where
\tilde{d}_{b}:\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{PV}}}^{\bullet}(R)\to\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{PV}}}^{\bullet}(R)[1]
is determined uniquely by the fact that the following diagram commutes

(8.3)

\hbox to256.58pt{\vbox to60.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 128.28694pt\lower-28.13167pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-13.2482pt}{-15.08586pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{65}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\ .

Although we will not use it, note that by Corollary
8.28
, we obtain the following:

Corollary 8.35
.

Suppose the underlying
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra
R=(M,m)
is very smooth. Then the inclusion of the subcomplex of internal variational Poisson cochains is a quasi-isomorphism,

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R)\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\bullet}(R)\ .

Moreover, we have the following comparison of the internal and global variational Poisson cochains on
R
, which is the first main result of this section:

Proposition 8.36
.

Suppose
X
is affine and the underlying
{\textup{Comm}}^{!}
algebra of
R
is very smooth and locally free as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
module. There is a natural isomorphism of objects in the derived category

h(\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R))\cong C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{PV}}}(R)\ .

Proof.

We begin by analyzing the underlying cohomologically graded vector space. Note that
\Omega^{1}_{R}
is a projective object of
{D}(R)
since
R
is very smooth, and thus
(\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p}\in{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})
is projective, so that we have

(8.4)

\Hom_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)\cong\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)\ .

Similarly, by Proposition
8.24
we have

(8.5)

{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)\cong{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)\ .

Thus, we can compute

\displaystyle\Hom_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\Hom_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong(\pi^{\times p})_{*}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong(\pi^{\times p})_{*}\Delta_{*}\Delta^{!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{*}\Delta^{!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{*}\Delta^{!}_{X}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{*}\Delta_{R}^{\bullet}\Delta^{!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{p},R)\ ,

where the first isomorphism follows from Equation
8.4
, the second from Proposition
8.17
, the third from Kashiwara’s lemma noting
{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{\textup{D}}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)
is supported on the diagonal, the fourth by naturality of pushforward, the fifth by Equation
8.24
, and the sixth since the object
\Delta^{!}{\mathcal{H}\textup{om}}_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},\Delta_{*}R)\in{D}(R^{\otimes^{!}p})
is supported scheme theoretically on the image of the diagonal embedding
\Delta_{R}:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\times p}
.

Now, we can explicitly compute the internal Hom object
\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{p},R)\in{D}(R)
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{p},R)

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{R}^{(p)\bullet}\Delta^{(p)!}(\left((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p}\right)^{\circ_{R^{\boxtimes p}}}\otimes^{!}_{R^{\boxtimes p}}\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\Delta_{R}^{(p)\bullet}\Delta^{!}((\Theta_{R})^{\boxtimes p}\otimes^{!}_{R^{\boxtimes p}}\Delta_{*}R)

\displaystyle\cong\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}\otimes^{!}_{R}R

\displaystyle\cong\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}\ ,

where the first isomorphism follows from Proposition
8.23
, the second by the compatibility of duality with the exterior product, and the third noting
\Delta^{!}
is symmetric monoidal and applying Kashiwara’s lemma.

Note that
\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}
is also projective as an object of
{D}(R)
, or equivalently as a module over
R[\mathcal{D}_{X}]
. Since
R
is locally free as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
module,
R[\mathcal{D}_{X}]
is locally free over
\mathcal{D}_{X}
and thus locally projective as an object of
{D}(X)
. Thus,
R[\mathcal{D}_{X}]
is locally (on X) a direct summand of a free
\mathcal{D}_{X}
module, and similarly
\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}
is globally a direct summand of a free
R[\mathcal{D}_{X}]
module, so that
\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}
is itself locally (on X) a direct summand of a free
\mathcal{D}_{X}
module.

In summary, we conclude that
\Theta_{R}^{\otimes^{!}_{R}p}
is itself locally projective in
{D}(X)
, and thus by Proposition
3.6
together with the preceding calculation, we have

\Hom_{{D}(R^{\boxtimes p})}((\Omega^{1}_{R})^{\boxtimes p},R)\cong\pi_{*}\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{p},R)\cong h(\mathcal{P}^{*}_{R,p}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{p},R))\ ,

and similarly for the modules of
{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{p}
-coinvariants. This completes the identification of the underlying cohomologically graded vector spaces, and the identification of the differentials follows by definition.
∎

8.5.
Variational cochains on symplectic coisson algebras

We begin with an alternative interpretation of the complex of internal variational cochains.

Let
R
be a commutative algebra object in
{\mathcal{D}}(X)^{!}
. Following section 1.4.18 of
[
BD04
]
, we have:

Proposition 8.37
.

A coisson structure
P\in P_{2}^{*}(\{R,R\},R)
is equivalent to a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid structure on
\Omega^{1}_{R}
such that the induced map

\tilde{P}:=\tau\circ(d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}\boxtimes\mathbbm{1}_{R})\in P_{2}^{*}(\{R,R\},R)

is skew-symmetric and satisfies
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}\circ\tilde{P}=b\circ(d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}\boxtimes d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}})\in P_{2}^{*}(\{R,R\},R)
, where
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}:R\to\Omega_{R}^{1}
is the de Rham differential on
\mathcal{Y}={\textup{Spec\ }}_{\mathcal{D}_{X}}R
.

Proof.

The
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket on
R
induced by
P
, together with the adjoint action of
R
on itself by derivations, makes
R\otimes R
into a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid on
\mathcal{Y}={\textup{Spec\ }}R
. The natural projection map
R\otimes R\to\Omega^{1}_{R}
defined by
r_{1}\otimes r_{2}\mapsto r_{1}\cdot d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}r_{2}
has kernel a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
ideal so that the bracket descends to
\Omega_{R}^{1}
. The induced map
\tilde{P}
is canonically identified with the underlying coisson structure
P
since the action
\tau
was defined in terms of
P
acting on a choice of representative, and such a choice is already made by precomposition with
d
. This satisfies the property that
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}:R\to\Omega^{1}_{R}
is a map of
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras, since we may compute the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket in the latter by choosing representatives of the form
1\otimes r
to compute the bracket on
\Omega_{R}
; this is equivalent to the condition
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}\circ\tilde{P}=b\circ(d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}\boxtimes d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}})
.

Conversely, the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid structure evidently determines a 2-ary
\tilde{P}
according to the formula, which is skew-symmetric by hypothesis, satisfies the Jacobi identity since
b
does, and acts by derivations on itself in the second argument since
\tau
acts by derivations, and therefore in the first argument by skew-symmetry.
∎

In particular, note that we have shown:

Corollary 8.38
.

Let
R
be a coisson algebra and
\mathcal{Y}={\textup{Spec\ }}R
. Then
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}:R\to\Omega^{1}_{R}
is a map of
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras, and the adjoint
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
module structure on
R
is the restriction along
d_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\textup{dR}}
of the action
\tau
of
\Omega^{1}_{R}
on
R
by derivations.

Given a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid
\mathcal{L}
on
R
, we make the following definition:

Definition 8.39
.

The (reduced) internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains
C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}},R}(\mathcal{L})
is defined by

C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}},R}(\mathcal{L})=\left(\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{R,n}^{*}(\{\mathcal{L}\}_{i=1}^{n},R)_{{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{n}}[1-n]\ ,\ d_{\text{CE,R}}\right)\ ,

where
d_{{\textup{CE}},R}:C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}},R}(\mathcal{L})\to C^{\bullet}_{{\textup{CE}},R}(\mathcal{L})[1]
is the Chevalley–Eilenberg differential determined by the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
bracket on
\mathcal{L}
and the action of
\mathcal{L}
on
R
by derivations.

For example, the internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on
\mathcal{L}=\Theta_{R}
with its canonical
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid structure gives the relative de Rham complex viewed as an object of
{\textup{D}}(X)
,

\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{CE}},R}^{\bullet}(\Theta_{R})=(\Omega^{\bullet\geq 1}_{\mathcal{Y}/X}\ ,\ d^{\textup{dR}}_{\mathcal{Y}})[1]\ .

Proposition 8.40
.

Let
R
be a coisson algebra. There is a canonical isomorphism of complexes

\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{PV}}}^{\bullet}(R)=\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{CE}},R}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{R})\ ,

where
\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{CE}},R}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{R})
the internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains with respect to the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid structure on
\Omega^{1}_{R}
induced by the coisson structure on
R
.

Proof.

Recall from Corollary
8.38
that the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebroid structure on
\Omega^{1}_{R}
determined by the coisson structure on
R
is defined so that
d_{R}^{\textup{dR}}:R\to\Omega^{1}_{R}
is a map of
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebras, and the restriction along
d_{R}^{\textup{dR}}
of the action of
\Omega^{1}_{R}
on
R
by derivations agrees with the adjoint action of the underlying
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra of
R
on itself.

Thus, by the functoriality of (reduced) internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra with coefficients in a
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
module, we obtain a natural map
\mathcal{C}_{\textup{CE}}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{R};R)\to\mathcal{C}_{\textup{CE}}^{\bullet}(R)
, defined by

\mathcal{P}_{n}^{*}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{n},R)\xrightarrow{(\cdot)\circ(d_{R}^{\textup{dR}})^{\boxtimes n}}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{*}(\{R\}_{i=1}^{n},R)

on each cohomologically graded component, where
\mathcal{C}_{\textup{CE}}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{R};R)
denotes the (reduced) internal Chevalley–Eilenberg cochains on
\Omega^{1}_{R}
viewed as a plain
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
algebra, with coefficients in the
{\textup{Lie}}^{*}
module
R
.

Further, note that by construction we have a natural map
\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{CE}},R}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1})\to\mathcal{C}_{\textup{CE}}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1}_{R};R)
defined by

\mathcal{P}_{R,n}^{*}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{n},R)\xrightarrow{{\textup{ob}}}\mathcal{P}_{n}^{*}(\{\Omega^{1}_{R}\}_{i=1}^{n},R)

on each cohomologically graded component, where
{\textup{ob}}:{D}(R)^{*}\to{D}(X)^{*}
denotes the natural forgetful functor.
Moreover, the composition of the preceding maps identifies
\mathcal{C}_{{\textup{CE}},R}^{\bullet}(\Omega^{1})
with a subcomplex of
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{\textup{CE}}(R)
, so that the differential
d_{{\textup{CE}},R}
on the former is uniquely determined by commutativity of the following diagram

\hbox to255.07pt{\vbox to57.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 127.53154pt\lower-27.93172pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
